href="https://als.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Thailand&amp;oldid=1088299">wiki</a>,<a href="https://wikimedia-pea.jimdofree.com/">ศาลปกครอง</a></td></tr></tbody></table></figure>
<!-- /wp:table -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p><strong>Git</strong>&nbsp;(<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English">/ɡɪt/</a>&nbsp;<a href="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/9/9a/En-uk-git.ogg/En-uk-git.ogg.mp3"></a><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:En-uk-git.ogg">ⓘ</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-:0-9">[8]</a></sup>) is a&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Distributed_version_control">distributed version control</a>&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software_system">software system</a><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChaconStraub201429%E2%80%9331-10">[9]</a></sup>&nbsp;that is capable of&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Version_control">managing versions</a>&nbsp;of source code or data. It is often used to control&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Source_code">source code</a>&nbsp;by&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Programmer">programmers</a>&nbsp;who are&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software_development">developing</a>&nbsp;software&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collaborative_development_environment">collaboratively</a>. It was originally created by&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linus_Torvalds">Linus Torvalds</a>&nbsp;for version control in the development of the&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linux_kernel">Linux kernel</a>.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-pro-git-1.2-11">[10]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Design goals of Git include speed,&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Data_integrity">data integrity</a>, and support for&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Distributed_computing">distributed</a>,&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-linear_editing">non-linear</a>&nbsp;workflows—thousands of parallel&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Branching_(version_control)">branches</a>&nbsp;running on different computers.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-kernel_SCM_saga-12">[11]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-integrity_goals-13">[12]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-linusGoogleTalk-14">[13]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>As with most other distributed version control systems, and unlike most&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Client%E2%80%93server">client–server</a>&nbsp;systems, Git maintains a local copy of the entire&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Repository_(version_control)">repository</a>, also known as the "repo", with history and version-tracking abilities, independent of&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computer_network">network</a>&nbsp;access or a central&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Server_(computing)">server</a>. A repository is stored on each computer in a standard&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Directory_(computing)">directory</a>&nbsp;with additional,&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hidden_file_and_hidden_directory">hidden files</a>&nbsp;to provide version control capabilities.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-15">[14]</a></sup>&nbsp;Git provides features to&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synchronization_(computer_science)">synchronize</a>&nbsp;changes between repositories that share history; for asynchronous collaboration, this extends to repositories on remote machines. Although all repositories (with the same history) are peers, developers often use a central server to host a repository to hold an integrated copy.</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Today, Git is the version control system most commonly used by software developers. It is the most popular distributed version control system,<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-16">[15]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-17">[16]</a></sup>&nbsp;with nearly 95% of developers reporting it as their primary version control system as of 2022.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-:1-18">[17]</a></sup>&nbsp;It is the most widely used source-code management tool among professional developers. Several different&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software_forge">software forges</a>&nbsp;offer Git repository services, including&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GitHub">GitHub</a>,&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SourceForge">SourceForge</a>,&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bitbucket">Bitbucket</a>&nbsp;and&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GitLab">GitLab</a>.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-Krill_2016-19">[18]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-Alexa_github.com-20">[19]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-Alexa_sourceforge.net-21">[20]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-Alexa_bitbucket.org-22">[21]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-Alexa_gitlab.com-23">[22]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:heading -->
<h2 class="wp-block-heading">History</h2>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Torvalds started developing Git in April 2005 after the free&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software_license">license</a>&nbsp;for&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BitKeeper">BitKeeper</a>, the proprietary&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Version_control">source-control management</a>&nbsp;(SCM) system used for Linux kernel development since 2002, was revoked for Linux.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-24">[23]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-linux.com441473-25">[24]</a></sup>&nbsp;The copyright holder of BitKeeper,&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larry_McVoy">Larry McVoy</a>, claimed that&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Tridgell">Andrew Tridgell</a>&nbsp;had created&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SourcePuller">SourcePuller</a>&nbsp;by&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reverse_engineering">reverse engineering</a>&nbsp;the BitKeeper&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communication_protocol">protocols</a>.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-26">[25]</a></sup>&nbsp;The same incident also spurred the creation of&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercurial">Mercurial</a>, another version-control system.</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Torvalds wanted a distributed system that he could use like BitKeeper, but none of the available free systems met his needs. He cited an example of a source-control management system needing 30 seconds to apply a patch and update all associated metadata, and noted that this would not scale to the needs of Linux kernel development, where synchronizing with fellow maintainers could require 250 such actions at once. For his design criterion, he specified that patching should take no more than three seconds, and added three more goals:<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-kernel_SCM_saga-12">[11]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:list {"anchor":"mwoA"} -->
<ul id="mwoA" class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>Take the&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concurrent_Versions_System">Concurrent Versions System</a>&nbsp;(CVS) as an example of what&nbsp;<em>not</em>&nbsp;to do; if in doubt, make the exact opposite decision.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-linusGoogleTalk-14">[13]</a></sup></li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>Support a distributed, BitKeeper-like workflow.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-linusGoogleTalk-14">[13]</a></sup></li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>Include very strong safeguards against corruption, either accidental or malicious.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-integrity_goals-13">[12]</a></sup></li>
<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
<!-- /wp:list -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>These criteria eliminated every version-control system in use at the time, so immediately after the 2.6.12-rc2 Linux kernel development release, Torvalds set out to write his own.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-linusGoogleTalk-14">[13]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>The development of Git began on 3 April 2005.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-selfhost-27">[26]</a></sup>&nbsp;Torvalds announced the project on 6 April and became&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-hosting_(compilers)">self-hosting</a>&nbsp;the next day.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-selfhost-27">[26]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-28">[27]</a></sup>&nbsp;The first merge of multiple branches took place on 18 April.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-29">[28]</a></sup>&nbsp;Torvalds achieved his performance goals; on 29 April, the nascent Git was benchmarked recording patches to the Linux kernel tree at a rate of 6.7 patches per second.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-30">[29]</a></sup>&nbsp;On 16 June, Git managed the kernel 2.6.12 release.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-31">[30]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Torvalds turned over&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software_maintenance">maintenance</a>&nbsp;on 26 July 2005 to Junio Hamano, a major contributor to the project.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-32">[31]</a></sup>&nbsp;Hamano was responsible for the 1.0 release on 21 December 2005.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-33">[32]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Naming</h3>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Torvalds quipped about the name&nbsp;<em><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git_(slang)">git</a></em>&nbsp;(which is&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_English">British English</a>&nbsp;slang for an unpleasant or silly person): "I'm an egotistical bastard, and I name all my projects after myself. First '<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linux_kernel">Linux</a>', now 'git'."<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-whythegitname-34">[33]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-35">[34]</a></sup>&nbsp;The&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Man_page">man page</a>&nbsp;describes Git as "the stupid content tracker".<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-36">[35]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>The&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/README">read-me file</a>&nbsp;of the source code elaborates further:<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-37">[36]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:quote -->
<blockquote class="wp-block-quote"><!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>"git" can mean anything, depending on your mood.</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:list -->
<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>Random three-letter combination that is pronounceable, and not actually used by any common UNIX command. The fact that it is a mispronunciation of "get" may or may not be relevant.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>Stupid. Contemptible and despicable. Simple. Take your pick from the dictionary of slang.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>"Global information tracker": you're in a good mood, and it actually works for you. Angels sing, and a light suddenly fills the room.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>"Goddamn idiotic truckload of sh*t": when it breaks.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
<!-- /wp:list --></blockquote>
<!-- /wp:quote -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>The source code for Git refers to the program as "the information manager from hell".<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-38">[37]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-39">[38]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:heading -->
<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Characteristics</h2>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Design</h3>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Git's design is a synthesis of Torvalds's experience with Linux in maintaining a large distributed development project, along with his intimate knowledge of file-system performance gained from the same project and the urgent need to produce a working system in short order. These influences led to the following implementation choices:<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-pro-git-1.2-11">[10]</a></sup>Strong support for non-linear developmentGit supports rapid branching and merging, and includes specific tools for visualizing and navigating a non-linear development history. In Git, a core assumption is that a change will be merged more often than it is written, as it is passed around to various reviewers. In Git, branches are very lightweight: a branch is only a reference to one commit.Distributed developmentLike&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darcs">Darcs</a>,&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BitKeeper">BitKeeper</a>,&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercurial">Mercurial</a>,&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GNU_Bazaar">Bazaar</a>, and&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monotone_(software)">Monotone</a>, Git gives each developer a local copy of the full development history, and changes are copied from one such repository to another. These changes are imported as added development branches and can be merged in the same way as a locally developed branch.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-40">[39]</a></sup>Compatibility with existing systems and protocolsRepositories can be published via&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HTTPS">Hypertext Transfer Protocol Secure</a>&nbsp;(HTTPS),&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypertext_Transfer_Protocol">Hypertext Transfer Protocol</a>&nbsp;(HTTP),&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File_Transfer_Protocol">File Transfer Protocol</a>&nbsp;(FTP), or a Git protocol over either a plain socket or&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secure_Shell">Secure Shell</a>&nbsp;(ssh). Git also has a CVS server emulation, which enables the use of existing CVS clients and IDE plugins to access Git repositories.&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apache_Subversion">Subversion</a>&nbsp;repositories can be used directly with git-svn.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-41">[40]</a></sup>Efficient handling of large projectsTorvalds has described Git as being very fast and scalable,<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-42">[41]</a></sup>&nbsp;and performance tests done by Mozilla<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-43">[42]</a></sup>&nbsp;showed that it was an&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_of_magnitude">order of magnitude</a>&nbsp;faster diffing large repositories than&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercurial">Mercurial</a>&nbsp;and&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GNU_Bazaar">GNU Bazaar</a>; fetching version history from a locally stored repository can be one hundred times faster than fetching it from the remote server.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-44">[43]</a></sup>Cryptographic authentication of historyThe Git history is stored in such a way that the ID of a particular version (a&nbsp;<em>commit</em>&nbsp;in Git terms) depends upon the complete development history leading up to that commit. Once it is published, it is not possible to change the old versions without it being noticed. The structure is similar to a&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merkle_tree">Merkle tree</a>, but with added data at the nodes and leaves.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-45">[44]</a></sup>&nbsp;(<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercurial">Mercurial</a>&nbsp;and&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monotone_(software)">Monotone</a>&nbsp;also have this property.)Toolkit-based designGit was designed as a set of programs written in&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C_(programming_language)">C</a>&nbsp;and several shell scripts that provide wrappers around those programs.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-46">[45]</a></sup>&nbsp;Although most of those scripts have since been rewritten in C for speed and portability, the design remains, and it is easy to chain the components together.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-47">[46]</a></sup>Pluggable merge strategiesAs part of its toolkit design, Git has a well-defined model of an incomplete merge, and it has multiple algorithms for completing it, culminating in telling the user that it is unable to complete the merge automatically and that manual editing is needed.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-48">[47]</a></sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garbage_(computer_science)">Garbage</a>&nbsp;accumulates until collectedAborting operations or backing out changes will leave useless dangling objects in the database. These are generally a small fraction of the continuously growing history of wanted objects. Git will automatically perform&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garbage_collection_(computer_science)">garbage collection</a>&nbsp;when enough loose objects have been created in the repository. Garbage collection can be called explicitly using&nbsp;<code>git gc</code>.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChaconStraub2014-49">[48]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-50">[49]</a></sup>Periodic explicit object packingGit stores each newly created object as a separate file. Although individually compressed, this takes up a great deal of space and is inefficient. This is solved by the use of&nbsp;<em>packs</em>&nbsp;that store a large number of objects&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delta_encoding">delta-compressed</a>&nbsp;among themselves in one file (or network byte stream) called a&nbsp;<em>packfile</em>. Packs are compressed using the&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heuristic_(computer_science)">heuristic</a>&nbsp;that files with the same name are probably similar, without depending on this for correctness. A corresponding index file is created for each packfile, recording the offset of each object in the packfile. Newly created objects (with newly added history) are still stored as single objects, and periodic repacking is needed to maintain space efficiency. The process of packing the repository can be very computationally costly. By allowing objects to exist in the repository in a loose but quickly generated format, Git allows the costly pack operation to be deferred until later, when time matters less, e.g., the end of a workday. Git does periodic repacking automatically, but manual repacking is also possible with the&nbsp;<code>git gc</code>&nbsp;command.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChaconStraub2014499-51">[50]</a></sup>&nbsp;For data integrity, both the packfile and its index have an&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SHA-1">SHA-1</a>&nbsp;checksum<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChaconStraub201433%E2%80%9334-52">[51]</a></sup>&nbsp;inside, and the file name of the packfile also contains an SHA-1 checksum. To check the integrity of a repository, run the&nbsp;<code>git fsck</code>&nbsp;command.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-Git_-_Packfiles-53">[52]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChaconStraub2014568-54">[53]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Another property of Git is that it snapshots directory trees of files. The earliest systems for tracking versions of source code,&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Source_Code_Control_System">Source Code Control System</a>&nbsp;(SCCS) and&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revision_Control_System">Revision Control System</a>&nbsp;(RCS), worked on individual files and emphasized the space savings to be gained from&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interleaved_deltas">interleaved deltas</a>&nbsp;(SCCS) or&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delta_encoding">delta encoding</a>&nbsp;(RCS) the (mostly similar) versions. Later revision-control systems maintained this notion of a file having an identity across multiple revisions of a project. However, Torvalds rejected this concept.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-55">[54]</a></sup>&nbsp;Consequently, Git does not explicitly record file revision relationships at any level below the source-code tree.</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:heading {"level":4} -->
<h4 class="wp-block-heading">Downsides</h4>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>These implicit revision relationships have some significant consequences:</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:list {"anchor":"mwAXw"} -->
<ul id="mwAXw" class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>It is slightly more costly to examine the change history of one file than the whole project.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-56">[55]</a></sup>&nbsp;To obtain a history of changes affecting a given file, Git must walk the global history and then determine whether each change modified that file. This method of examining history does, however, let Git produce with equal efficiency a single history showing the changes to an arbitrary set of files. For example, a subdirectory of the source tree plus an associated global header file is a very common case.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>Renames are handled implicitly rather than explicitly. A common complaint with&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concurrent_Versions_System">CVS</a>&nbsp;is that it uses the name of a file to identify its revision history, so moving or renaming a file is not possible without either interrupting its history or renaming the history and thereby making the history inaccurate. Most post-CVS revision-control systems solve this by giving a file a unique long-lived name (analogous to an&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inode">inode</a>&nbsp;number) that survives renaming. Git does not record such an identifier, and this is claimed as an advantage.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-57">[56]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-58">[57]</a></sup>&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Source_code">Source code</a>&nbsp;files are sometimes split or merged, or simply renamed,<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-59">[58]</a></sup>&nbsp;and recording this as a simple rename would freeze an inaccurate description of what happened in the (immutable) history. Git addresses the issue by detecting renames while browsing the history of snapshots rather than recording it when making the snapshot.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-60">[59]</a></sup>&nbsp;(Briefly, given a file in revision&nbsp;<em>N</em>, a file of the same name in revision&nbsp;<em>N</em>&nbsp;−&nbsp;1 is its default ancestor. However, when there is no like-named file in revision&nbsp;<em>N</em>&nbsp;−&nbsp;1, Git searches for a file that existed only in revision&nbsp;<em>N</em>&nbsp;−&nbsp;1 and is very similar to the new file.) However, it does require more&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CPU">CPU</a>-intensive work every time the history is reviewed, and several options to adjust the heuristics are available. This mechanism does not always work; sometimes a file that is renamed with changes in the same commit is read as a deletion of the old file and the creation of a new file. Developers can work around this limitation by committing the rename and the changes separately.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
<!-- /wp:list -->

<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Merging strategies</h3>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Git implements several merging strategies; a non-default strategy can be selected at merge time:<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-61">[60]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:list {"anchor":"mwAac"} -->
<ul id="mwAac" class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><em>resolve</em>: the traditional&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three-way_merge">three-way merge</a>&nbsp;algorithm.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><em>recursive</em>: This is the default when pulling or merging one branch, and is a variant of the three-way merge algorithm.When there are more than one common ancestors that can be used for a three-way merge, it creates a merged tree of the common ancestors and uses that as the reference tree for the three-way merge. This has been reported to result in fewer merge conflicts without causing mis-merges by tests done on prior merge commits taken from Linux 2.6 kernel development history. Also, this can detect and handle merges involving renames.— Linus Torvalds<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-62">[61]</a></sup></li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><em>octopus</em>: This is the default when merging more than two heads.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
<!-- /wp:list -->

<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Data structures</h3>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Git's primitives are not inherently a&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Version_control">source-code management</a>&nbsp;system. Torvalds explains:<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-63">[62]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:quote -->
<blockquote class="wp-block-quote"><!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>In many ways you can just see git as a filesystem—it's&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Content-addressable_storage">content-addressable</a>, and it has a notion of versioning, but I really designed it coming at the problem from the viewpoint of a&nbsp;<em>filesystem</em>&nbsp;person (hey, kernels is what I do), and I actually have absolutely&nbsp;<em>zero</em>&nbsp;interest in creating a traditional SCM system.</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph --></blockquote>
<!-- /wp:quote -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>From this initial design approach, Git has developed the full set of features expected of a traditional SCM,<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-bare_url-64">[63]</a></sup>&nbsp;with features mostly being created as needed, then refined and extended over time.</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:image {"width":"250px","height":"auto","aspectRatio":"1.366120218579235","linkDestination":"custom","anchor":"mwAcM"} -->
<figure class="wp-block-image is-resized" id="mwAcM"><a class="mw-file-description" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Git_operations.svg"><img src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Git_operations.svg/250px-Git_operations.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" alt="" style="aspect-ratio:1.366120218579235;width:250px;height:auto" /></a><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Some data flows and storage levels in the Git revision control system</figcaption></figure>
<!-- /wp:image -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Git has two&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Data_structure">data structures</a>: a mutable&nbsp;<em>index</em>&nbsp;(also called&nbsp;<em>stage</em>&nbsp;or&nbsp;<em>cache</em>) that caches information about the&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Working_directory">working directory</a>&nbsp;and the next revision to be committed; and an&nbsp;<em>object database</em>&nbsp;that stores immutable objects.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-:3-65">[64]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>The index serves as a connection point between the&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Object_database">object database</a>&nbsp;and the working tree.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-:3-65">[64]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>The&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Object_store">object store</a>&nbsp;contains five types of objects:<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-66">[65]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-Git_-_Packfiles-53">[52]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:list {"anchor":"mwAeI"} -->
<ul id="mwAeI" class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>A&nbsp;<strong>blob</strong>&nbsp;is the content of a&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computer_file">file</a>. Blobs have no proper file name, time stamps, or other metadata (a blob's name internally is a hash of its content). In Git, each blob is a version of a file, in which is the file's data.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChaconStraub201481%E2%80%9383-67">[66]</a></sup></li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>A&nbsp;<strong>tree</strong>&nbsp;object is the equivalent of a directory. It contains a list of file names,<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChaconStraub2014485%E2%80%93488-68">[67]</a></sup>&nbsp;each with some type bits and a reference to a blob or tree object that is that file, symbolic link, or directory's contents. These objects are a snapshot of the source tree. (In whole, this comprises a&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merkle_tree">Merkle tree</a>, meaning that only a single hash for the root tree is sufficient and actually used in commits to precisely pinpoint to the exact state of whole tree structures of any number of sub-directories and files.)</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>A&nbsp;<strong>commit</strong>&nbsp;object links tree objects together into history. It contains the name of a tree object (of the top-level source directory), a timestamp, a log message, and the names of zero or more parent commit objects.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChaconStraub2014488%E2%80%93490-69">[68]</a></sup></li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>A&nbsp;<strong>tag</strong>&nbsp;object is a container that contains a reference to another object and can hold added meta-data related to another object. Most commonly, it is used to store a&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_signature">digital signature</a>&nbsp;of a commit object corresponding to a particular release of the data being tracked by Git.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChaconStraub2014495%E2%80%93496-70">[69]</a></sup></li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>A&nbsp;<strong>packfile</strong>&nbsp;object collects various other objects into a&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zlib">zlib</a>-compressed bundle for compactness and ease of transport over network protocols.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChaconStraub2014497%E2%80%93501-71">[70]</a></sup></li>
<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
<!-- /wp:list -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Each object is identified by a SHA-1&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cryptographic_hash_function">hash</a>&nbsp;of its contents. Git computes the hash and uses this value for the object's name. The object is put into a directory matching the first two characters of its hash. The rest of the hash is used as the file name for that object.</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Git stores each revision of a file as a unique blob. The relationships between the blobs can be found through examining the tree and commit objects. Newly added objects are stored in their entirety using zlib compression. This can consume a large amount of disk space quickly, so objects can be combined into&nbsp;<em>packs</em>, which use&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delta_encoding">delta compression</a>&nbsp;to save space, storing blobs as their changes relative to other blobs.</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Additionally, Git stores labels called refs (short for references) to indicate the locations of various commits. They are stored in the reference database and are respectively:<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-72">[71]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:list {"anchor":"mwAg8"} -->
<ul id="mwAg8" class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><strong>Heads (branches)</strong>: Named references that are advanced automatically to the new commit when a commit is made on top of them.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><strong>HEAD</strong>: A reserved head that will be compared against the working tree to create a commit.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><strong>Tags</strong>: Like branch references, but fixed to a particular commit. Used to label important points in history.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
<!-- /wp:list -->

<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Commands</h3>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Frequently used commands for Git's&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Command-line_interface">command-line interface</a>&nbsp;include:<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-73">[72]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-74">[73]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:list {"anchor":"mwAiE"} -->
<ul id="mwAiE" class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><code>git init</code>, which is used to create a git repository.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><code>git clone [URL]</code>, which&nbsp;<em>clones</em>, or duplicates, a git repository from an external URL.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><code>git add [file]</code>, which adds a file to git's&nbsp;<em>index</em>&nbsp;(files about to be committed).</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><code>git commit -m [commit message]</code>, which&nbsp;<em>commits</em>&nbsp;the files from the index (staging area) as a new snapshot, so they are now part of the repository's history.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
<!-- /wp:list -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>A&nbsp;<em>.gitignore</em>&nbsp;file may be created in a Git repository as a plain&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Text_file">text file</a>. The files listed in the&nbsp;<em>.gitignore</em>&nbsp;file will&nbsp;<em>not</em>&nbsp;be tracked by Git.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-75">[74]</a></sup><sup>: 3–4 </sup>&nbsp;This feature can be used to ignore files with keys or passwords, various extraneous files, and large files (which&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GitHub">GitHub</a>&nbsp;will refuse to upload).<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-76">[75]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Git references</h3>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Every object in the Git database that is not referred to may be cleaned up by using a garbage collection command or automatically. An object may be referenced by another object or an explicit reference. Git has different types of references. The commands to create, move, and delete references vary.&nbsp;<code>git show-ref</code>&nbsp;lists all references. Some types are:</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:list {"anchor":"mwAj8"} -->
<ul id="mwAj8" class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><em>heads</em>: refers to an object locally,</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><em>remotes</em>: refers to an object which exists in a remote repository,</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><em>stash</em>: refers to an object not yet committed,</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><em>meta</em>:&nbsp;<em>e.g.</em>, a configuration in a bare repository, user rights; the refs/meta/config namespace was introduced retrospectively, gets used by&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerrit_(software)">Gerrit</a>,<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-Gerrit_Code_Review-77">[76]</a></sup></li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><em>tags</em>: see above.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
<!-- /wp:list -->

<!-- wp:heading -->
<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Implementations</h2>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:image {"width":"250px","height":"auto","aspectRatio":"1.4204545454545454","linkDestination":"custom","anchor":"mwAlE"} -->
<figure class="wp-block-image is-resized" id="mwAlE"><a class="mw-file-description" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:GNOME_gitg.png"><img src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/GNOME_gitg.png/250px-GNOME_gitg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" alt="" style="aspect-ratio:1.4204545454545454;width:250px;height:auto" /></a><figcaption class="wp-element-caption"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GNOME_Core_Applications">gitg</a>&nbsp;is a graphical front-end using&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GTK+">GTK+</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<!-- /wp:image -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Git (the main implementation in C) is primarily developed on&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linux">Linux</a>, although it also supports most major operating systems, including the BSDs (<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DragonFly_BSD">DragonFly BSD</a>,&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FreeBSD">FreeBSD</a>,&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NetBSD">NetBSD</a>, and&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OpenBSD">OpenBSD</a>),&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solaris_(operating_system)">Solaris</a>,&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MacOS">macOS</a>, and&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microsoft_Windows">Windows</a>.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-78">[77]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-79">[78]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>The first Windows&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porting">port</a>&nbsp;of Git was primarily a Linux-emulation framework that hosts the Linux version. Installing Git under Windows creates a similarly named Program Files directory containing the&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mingw-w64">Mingw-w64</a>&nbsp;port of the&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GNU_Compiler_Collection">GNU Compiler Collection</a>,&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perl">Perl</a>&nbsp;5,&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MSYS2">MSYS2</a>&nbsp;(itself a fork of&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cygwin">Cygwin</a>, a Unix-like emulation environment for Windows) and various other Windows ports or emulations of Linux utilities and libraries. Currently, native Windows builds of Git are distributed as 32- and 64-bit installers.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-80">[79]</a></sup>&nbsp;The git official website currently maintains a build of Git for Windows, still using the MSYS2 environment.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-81">[80]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>The JGit implementation of Git is a pure&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Java_(software_platform)">Java</a>&nbsp;software library, designed to be embedded in any Java application. JGit is used in the&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerrit_(software)">Gerrit</a>&nbsp;code-review tool, and in EGit, a Git client for the&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eclipse_(software)">Eclipse</a>&nbsp;IDE.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-82">[81]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Go-git is an&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open-source_software">open-source</a>&nbsp;implementation of Git written in pure&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Go_(programming_language)">Go</a>.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-83">[82]</a></sup>&nbsp;It is currently used for backing projects as a&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SQL">SQL</a>&nbsp;interface for Git code repositories<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-84">[83]</a></sup>&nbsp;and providing&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encryption">encryption</a>&nbsp;for Git.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-85">[84]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Dulwich is an implementation of Git written in pure&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programming_language)">Python</a>&nbsp;with support for CPython 3.6 and later and Pypy.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-86">[85]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>The libgit2 implementation of Git is an ANSI C software library with no other dependencies, which can be built on multiple platforms, including Windows, Linux, macOS, and BSD.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-87">[86]</a></sup>&nbsp;It has bindings for many programming languages, including&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruby_(programming_language)">Ruby</a>, Python, and&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haskell">Haskell</a>.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-88">[87]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-89">[88]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-90">[89]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Other implementations of Git include JS-Git (a&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JavaScript">JavaScript</a>&nbsp;implementation of a subset of Git),<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-91">[90]</a></sup>&nbsp;Game of Trees (an open-source implementation of Git for the&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OpenBSD">OpenBSD</a>&nbsp;project),<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-92">[91]</a></sup>&nbsp;git9 (an independent implementation of Git for&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plan_9_from_Bell_Labs">Plan 9</a>&nbsp;using the system native abstractions),<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-93">[92]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-94">[93]</a></sup>&nbsp;and gitoxide (an implementation of Git written in pure&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rust_(programming_language)">Rust</a>).<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-95">[94]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:heading -->
<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Git server</h2>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:image {"width":"250px","height":"auto","aspectRatio":"1.5527950310559007","linkDestination":"custom","anchor":"mwAsM"} -->
<figure class="wp-block-image is-resized" id="mwAsM"><a class="mw-file-description" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Gitweb.png"><img src="https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/Gitweb.png/250px-Gitweb.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" alt="" style="aspect-ratio:1.5527950310559007;width:250px;height:auto" /></a><figcaption class="wp-element-caption">Screenshot of gitweb interface showing a commit&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diff">diff</a></figcaption></figure>
<!-- /wp:image -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>As Git is a distributed version control system, it could be used as a server out of the box. It is shipped with a built-in command&nbsp;<code>git daemon</code>&nbsp;which starts a simple TCP server running on the Git protocol.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChaconStraub2014138%E2%80%93139-96">[95]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-97">[96]</a></sup>&nbsp;Dedicated Git HTTP servers help (amongst other features) by adding access control, displaying the contents of a Git repository via the web interfaces, and managing multiple repositories. Already existing Git repositories can be cloned and shared to be used by others as a centralized repo. It can also be accessed via remote shell just by having the Git software installed and allowing a user to log in.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-98">[97]</a></sup>&nbsp;Git servers typically listen on&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_(computer_networking)">TCP port</a>&nbsp;9418.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-99">[98]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>For Web-based repository browsing, Git is also shipped with gitweb, a CGI-based interface for viewing repository contents over HTTP.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-100">[99]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Open source</h3>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:list {"anchor":"mwAuE"} -->
<ul id="mwAuE" class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>Hosting the Git server using the Git Binary.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-101">[100]</a></sup></li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerrit_(software)">Gerrit</a>, a Git server configurable to support code reviews and provide access via ssh, an integrated&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apache_MINA">Apache MINA</a>&nbsp;or OpenSSH, or an integrated&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jetty_(web_server)">Jetty</a>&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web_server">web server</a>. Gerrit provides integration for LDAP, Active Directory, OpenID, OAuth, Kerberos/GSSAPI, X509 https client certificates. With Gerrit 3.0 all configurations will be stored as Git repositories, and no database is required to run. Gerrit has a pull-request feature implemented in its core but lacks a GUI for it.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phabricator">Phabricator</a>, a spin-off from Facebook. As Facebook primarily uses&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercurial">Mercurial</a>, Git support is not as prominent.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-102">[101]</a></sup></li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RhodeCode">RhodeCode</a>&nbsp;Community Edition (CE), supporting Git,&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercurial">Mercurial</a>&nbsp;and&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apache_Subversion">Subversion</a>&nbsp;with an&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AGPLv3">AGPLv3 license</a>.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kallithea_(software)">Kallithea</a>, supporting both Git and&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercurial">Mercurial</a>, developed in&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programming_language)">Python</a>&nbsp;with&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GPL_license">GPL license</a>.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>External projects like gitolite,<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-103">[102]</a></sup>&nbsp;which provide scripts on top of Git software to provide fine-grained access control.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>There are several other FLOSS solutions for self-hosting, including Gogs,<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-104">[103]</a></sup>&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gitea">Gitea</a>, a fork of Gogs, as well as&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forgejo">Forgejo</a>, which is, in turn, a fork of Gitea. Gogs, as well as the two aforementioned derivatives of it, is developed using the&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Go_(programming_language)">Go language</a>. All three solutions are made available under the&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MIT_license">MIT license</a>.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
<!-- /wp:list -->

<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Git server as a service</h3>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>See also:&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comparison_of_source-code-hosting_facilities">Comparison of source-code-hosting facilities</a></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>There are many offerings of Git repositories as a service. The most popular are&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GitHub">GitHub</a>,&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SourceForge">SourceForge</a>,&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bitbucket">Bitbucket</a>&nbsp;and&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GitLab">GitLab</a>.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-:2-105">[104]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-Alexa_github.com-20">[19]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-Alexa_sourceforge.net-21">[20]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-Alexa_bitbucket.org-22">[21]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-Alexa_gitlab.com-23">[22]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:heading -->
<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Graphical interfaces</h2>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Git GUI clients offer a graphical user interface (GUI) to simplify interaction with Git repositories.</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>These GUIs provide visual representations of project history, including branches, commits, and file changes. They also streamline actions like staging changes, creating commits, and managing branches. Visual diff tools help resolve merge conflicts arising from concurrent development.</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Git comes with a&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tcl/Tk">Tcl/Tk</a>&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graphical_user_interface">GUI</a>, which allows users to perform actions such as creating and amending commits, creating and merging branches, and interacting with remote repositories.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-106">[105]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>In addition to the official GUI, many third-party interfaces exist that provide similar features to the official GUI distributed with Git.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-107">[106]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>GUI clients make Git easier to learn and use, improving workflow efficiency and reducing errors.</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:heading -->
<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Adoption</h2>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>The&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eclipse_Foundation">Eclipse Foundation</a>&nbsp;reported in its annual community survey that&nbsp;as of May&nbsp;2014, Git was the most widely used source-code management tool, with 42.9% of professional software developers reporting that they use Git as their primary source-control system<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-ianskerrett.wordpress.com-108">[107]</a></sup>&nbsp;compared with 36.3% in 2013, 32% in 2012; or for Git responses excluding use of&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GitHub">GitHub</a>: 33.3% in 2014, 30.3% in 2013, 27.6% in 2012 and 12.8% in 2011.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-109">[108]</a></sup>&nbsp;Open-source directory&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_Hub">Open Hub</a>&nbsp;reported a similar uptake among open-source projects.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-110">[109]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stack_Overflow">Stack Overflow</a>&nbsp;has included&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Version_control">version control</a>&nbsp;in their annual developer survey<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-111">[110]</a></sup>&nbsp;in 2015 (16,694 responses),<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-112">[111]</a></sup>&nbsp;2017 (30,730 responses),<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-113">[112]</a></sup>&nbsp;2018 (74,298 responses)<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-114">[113]</a></sup>&nbsp;and 2022 (71,379 responses).<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-:1-18">[17]</a></sup>&nbsp;Git was the overwhelming favorite of responding developers in these surveys, reporting as high as 93.9% in 2022.</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Version control systems used by responding developers:</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:table -->
<figure class="wp-block-table"><table class="has-fixed-layout"><thead><tr><th class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">Name</th><th class="has-text-align-center" <!-- /wp:table -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>The UK IT jobs website itjobswatch.co.uk reports that as of late September 2016, 29.27% of UK permanent software development job openings have cited Git,<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-116">[114]</a></sup>&nbsp;ahead of 12.17% for Microsoft&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Team_Foundation_Server">Team Foundation Server</a>,<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-117">[115]</a></sup>&nbsp;10.60% for&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apache_Subversion">Subversion</a>,<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-118">[116]</a></sup>&nbsp;1.30% for&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercurial">Mercurial</a>,<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-119">[117]</a></sup>&nbsp;and 0.48% for&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microsoft_Visual_SourceSafe">Visual SourceSafe</a>.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-120">[118]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:heading {"level":3} -->
<h3 class="wp-block-heading">Extensions</h3>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>There are many&nbsp;<em>Git extensions</em>, like Git LFS, which started as an extension to Git in the GitHub community and is now widely used by other repositories. Extensions are usually independently developed and maintained by different people, but at some point in the future, a widely used extension can be merged with Git.</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Other open-source Git extensions include:</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:list {"anchor":"mwBCI"} -->
<ul id="mwBCI" class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git-annex">git-annex</a>, a distributed file synchronization system based on Git</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git-flow?action=edit&amp;redlink=1">git-flow</a>, a set of Git extensions to provide high-level repository operations for Vincent Driessen's branching model</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>git-machete, a repository organizer &amp; tool for automating rebase/merge/pull/push operations</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
<!-- /wp:list -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Microsoft developed the&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virtual_File_System_for_Git">Virtual File System for Git</a>&nbsp;(VFS for Git; formerly Git Virtual File System or GVFS) extension to handle the size of the&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microsoft_Windows">Windows</a>&nbsp;source-code tree as part of their 2017 migration from&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perforce">Perforce</a>. VFS for Git allows cloned repositories to use placeholders whose contents are downloaded only once a file is accessed.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-ars-windowsgit-121">[119]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:heading -->
<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Conventions</h2>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Git can be used in a variety of different ways, but some conventions are commonly adopted.</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:list {"anchor":"mwBDI"} -->
<ul id="mwBDI" class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>The command to create a local repo,&nbsp;<em>git init</em>, creates a branch named&nbsp;<em>master</em>.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChaconStraub201481%E2%80%9383-67">[66]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-122">[120]</a></sup>&nbsp;Often it is used as the integration branch for merging changes into.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-123">[121]</a></sup>&nbsp;Some tools, such as GitHub<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-124">[122]</a></sup>&nbsp;and GitLab,<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-125">[123]</a></sup>&nbsp;create a default branch named&nbsp;<em>main</em>&nbsp;instead;&nbsp;Git will start using&nbsp;<em>main</em>&nbsp;from the 3.0 release, expected by the end of 2026.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-126">[124]</a></sup>&nbsp;Since the default upstream remote is named&nbsp;<em>origin</em>,<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChaconStraub2014103%E2%80%93109-127">[125]</a></sup>&nbsp;the default remote branch is&nbsp;<em>origin/master</em>. Also, users can add and delete branches and choose any branch for integrating.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>Pushed commits generally are not overwritten, but are&nbsp;<em>reverted</em><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-128">[126]</a></sup>&nbsp;by committing another change which reverses an earlier commit. This prevents shared commits from being invalid because the commit on which they are based does not exist in the remote. If the commits contain sensitive information, they should be removed, which involves a more complex procedure to rewrite history.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>The&nbsp;<em>git-flow</em><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-129">[127]</a></sup>&nbsp;workflow and naming conventions are often adopted to distinguish feature-specific unstable histories (feature/*), unstable shared histories (develop), production-ready histories (main), and emergency patches to released products (hotfix).</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>A&nbsp;<em>pull request</em>, a.k.a.&nbsp;<em>merge request</em>, is a request by a user to merge a branch into another branch.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChaconStraub2014170%E2%80%93174-130">[128]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-131">[129]</a></sup>&nbsp;Git does not provide for pull requests, but it is a common feature of git cloud services. The underlying function of a pull request is no different than that of an administrator of a repository pulling changes from another remote (the repository that is the source of the pull request). However, the pull request is a ticket managed by the hosting server, which perform these actions; it is not a feature of git SCM.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
<!-- /wp:list -->

<!-- wp:heading -->
<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Security</h2>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Git does not provide access-control mechanisms, but was designed for operation with other tools that specialize in access control.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-132">[130]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>On 17 December 2014, an exploit was found affecting the&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microsoft_Windows">Windows</a>&nbsp;and&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MacOS">macOS</a>&nbsp;versions of the Git client. An attacker could perform&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arbitrary_code_execution">arbitrary code execution</a>&nbsp;on a target computer with Git installed by creating a malicious Git tree (directory) named&nbsp;<em>.git</em>&nbsp;(a directory in Git repositories that stores all the data of the repository) in a different case (such as .GIT or .Git, needed because Git does not allow the all-lowercase version of&nbsp;<em>.git</em>&nbsp;to be created manually) with malicious files in the&nbsp;<em>.git/hooks</em>&nbsp;subdirectory (a folder with executable files that Git runs) on a repository that the attacker made or on a repository that the attacker can modify. If a Windows or Mac user&nbsp;<em>pulls</em>&nbsp;(downloads) a version of the repository with the malicious directory, then switches to that directory, the .git directory will be overwritten (due to the case-insensitive trait of the Windows and Mac filesystems) and the malicious executable files in&nbsp;<em>.git/hooks</em>&nbsp;may be run, which results in the attacker's commands being executed. An attacker could also modify the&nbsp;<em>.git/config</em>&nbsp;configuration file, which allows the attacker to create malicious Git aliases (aliases for Git commands or external commands) or modify extant aliases to execute malicious commands when run. The vulnerability was patched in version 2.2.1 of Git, released on 17 December 2014, and announced the next day.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-133">[131]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-134">[132]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Git version 2.6.1, released on 29 September 2015, contained a patch for a security vulnerability (CVE-2015-7545)<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-135">[133]</a></sup>&nbsp;that allowed arbitrary code execution.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-git-v2.6.1-commit-136">[134]</a></sup>&nbsp;The vulnerability was exploitable if an attacker could convince a victim to clone a specific URL, as the arbitrary commands were embedded in the URL.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-seclists-CVE-2015-7545-137">[135]</a></sup>&nbsp;An attacker could use the exploit via a&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Man-in-the-middle_attack">man-in-the-middle attack</a>&nbsp;if the connection was unencrypted,<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-seclists-CVE-2015-7545-137">[135]</a></sup>&nbsp;as they could redirect the user to a URL of their choice. Recursive clones were also vulnerable, since they allowed the controller of a repository to specify arbitrary URLs via the gitmodules file.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-seclists-CVE-2015-7545-137">[135]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Git uses&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SHA-1">SHA-1</a>&nbsp;hashes internally. Linus Torvalds has responded that the hash was mostly to guard against accidental corruption, and the security a&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cryptographic_hash_function">cryptographically secure hash</a>&nbsp;gives was just an accidental side effect, with the main security being&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_signature">signing</a>&nbsp;elsewhere.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-138">[136]</a></sup><sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-139">[137]</a></sup>&nbsp;Since a demonstration of the&nbsp;<a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SHAttered">SHAttered</a>&nbsp;attack against Git in 2017, Git was modified to use an SHA-1 variant resistant to this attack. A plan for hash function transition is being written since February 2020.<sup><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Git#cite_note-140">[138]</a></sup></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<li>&nbsp;<a href="https://gitolite.com/gitolite/index.html">"Gitolite: Hosting Git Repositories"</a>.&nbsp;<a href="https://web.archive.org/web/20250613033744/https://gitolite.com/gitolite/index.html">Archived</a>&nbsp;from the original on 13 June 2025. Retrieved&nbsp;25 August&nbsp;2025.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>&nbsp;<a href="https://gogs.io/">"Gogs: A painless self-hosted Git service"</a>.&nbsp;<a href="https://web.archive.org/web/20250725193147/https://gogs.io/">Archived</a>&nbsp;from the original on 25 July 2025. Retrieved&nbsp;25 August&nbsp;2025.</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li>&nbsp;<a href="https://github.blog/2020-03-22-highlights-from-git-2-26/">"Highlights from Git 2.26"</a>.&nbsp;<em>The GitHub Blog</em>. 22 March 2020.&nbsp;<a href="https://web.archive.org/web/20210322043004/https://github.blog/2020-03-22-highlights-from-git-2-26/">Archived</a>&nbsp;from the original on 22 March 2021. Retrieved&nbsp;25 November&nbsp;2020.&nbsp;<q>You may remember when Git introduced a new version of its network fetch protocol way back in 2018. That protocol is now used by default in 2.26, so let's refresh ourselves on what that means. The biggest problem with the old protocol is that the server would immediately list all of the branches, tags, and other references in the repository before the client had a chance to send anything. For some repositories, this could mean sending megabytes of extra data, when the client really only wanted to know about the master branch. The new protocol starts with the client request and provides a way for the client to tell the server which references it's interested in. Fetching a single branch will only ask about that branch, while most clones will only ask about branches and tags. This might seem like everything, but server repositories may store other references (such as the head of every pull request opened in the repository since its creation). Now, fetches from large repositories improve in speed, especially when the fetch itself is small, which makes the cost of the initial reference advertisement more expensive relatively speaking. And the best part is that you won't need to do anything! Due to some clever design, any client that speaks the new protocol can work seamlessly with both old and new servers, falling back to the original protocol if the server doesn't support it. The only reason for the delay between introducing the protocol and making it the default was to let early adopters discover any bugs.</q></li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p><a href="https://archive.org/search?query=mediatype:collection&amp;sort=-downloads"></a><a href="https://archive.org/search?query=mediatype:collection&amp;sort=-downloads"></a></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p><strong>All-time views:&nbsp;</strong>318M</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p><strong>Collections:&nbsp;</strong><a href="https://archive.org/details/wikipediaoutlinks">Wikipedia Outlinks</a>,&nbsp;<a href="https://archive.org/details/webwidecrawl">Internet Archive Web Crawls</a>,&nbsp;<a href="https://archive.org/details/web">Web Crawls</a>,&nbsp;<a href="https://archive.org/details/wikicollections">Wiki Collections</a></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Crawl of outlinks from wikipedia.org started May, 2011. These files are currently not publicly accessible.</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>จําเป็นต้องมีบัญชีผู้ใช้เพื่อแก้ไขวิกินี้ แต่เราต้องปิดใช้งานการลงทะเบียนผู้ใช้สาธารณะเนื่องจากสแปม</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>หากต้องการขอบัญชี โปรดสอบถามผู้ใช้ที่ยืนยันอัตโนมัติที่&nbsp;<a rel="noopener" href="https://whatwg.org/chat" target="_blank">แชท</a>&nbsp;(เช่นหนึ่งใน&nbsp;<a href="https://wiki.whatwg.org/index.php?title=Special:ListUsers&amp;group=autoconfirmed">สมาชิกที่ยืนยันอัตโนมัติถาวรเหล่านี้</a>)</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:heading {"level":1} -->
<h1 class="wp-block-heading">MetaExtensions</h1>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p><a href="https://wiki.whatwg.org/wiki/MetaExtensions#mw-head">กระโดดไปที่การนําทาง</a><a href="https://wiki.whatwg.org/wiki/MetaExtensions#searchInput">กระโดดไปค้นหา</a></p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>หน้านี้แสดงรายการค่าส่วนขยายสําหรับแอตทริบิวต์ name="" ขององค์ประกอบ HTML &lt;meta&gt; คุณสามารถเพิ่มค่าของคุณเองลงในรายการนี้ได้</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>หากต้องการความช่วยเหลือในการจัดทําข้อกําหนด โปรดดูที่&nbsp;<a href="https://wiki.whatwg.org/wiki/Category:Specification_editing">การแก้ไขข้อกําหนด</a>&nbsp;หมวดหมู่และ&nbsp;<a rel="noopener" href="https://github.com/tabatkins/bikeshed/" target="_blank">โรงจักรยาน</a>&nbsp;เอกสารประกอบ</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>หากสเปคพร้อมเราขอให้คุณ:</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:list -->
<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><strong>หลีกเลี่ยงความซ้ําซ้อน</strong>&nbsp;หากมีคนกําหนดชื่อที่ใกล้เคียงกับที่คุณต้องการแล้ว โปรดนําชื่อนั้นกลับมาใช้ใหม่</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><strong>อย่าลืมรวม&nbsp;<em>ทั้งหมด</em>&nbsp;รายการ</strong>&nbsp;<a href="http://www.whatwg.org/specs/web-apps/current-work/multipage/semantics.html#other-metadata-names" target="_blank" rel="noopener">ระบุไว้ในข้อมูลจําเพาะ</a>&nbsp;<em>รวมถึงลิงก์ไปยังข้อกําหนด</em>&nbsp;ที่ระบุคีย์เวิร์ด&nbsp;<em>เป็นคําหลักเมตา HTML</em>● หากข้อเสนอขาดข้อกําหนดและมีเวอร์ชันในข้อกําหนดเฉพาะที่สมบูรณ์ ข้อเสนอหลังจะเป็นที่ต้องการ</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><strong>เรียงตามตัวอักษรตามคําหลัก</strong>, โดยไม่คํานึงถึงกรณี, ในตารางใด ๆ เรียงลําดับที่ไม่ใช่ตัวอักษรและตัวเลขตามค่า ASCII</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><strong>ไม่จองเนมสเปซ</strong>&nbsp;ให้ลงทะเบียนชื่อแต่ละชื่อแทน (เรียกว่าค่าที่แจกแจง แม้ว่าคุณจะไม่ต้องนับจํานวนก็ตาม)</li>
<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
<!-- /wp:list -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>ต้องไม่เพิ่มแอตทริบิวต์ที่มีค่า URL ลงในหน้านี้เป็นชื่อเมตา แต่ควรลงทะเบียนเป็น&nbsp;<a rel="noopener" href="http://microformats.org/wiki/existing-rel-values#HTML5_link_type_extensions" target="_blank">คําหลัก rel</a>&nbsp;แทน</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:heading -->
<h2 class="wp-block-heading">เนื้อหา</h2>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:list -->
<ul class="wp-block-list"><!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><a href="https://wiki.whatwg.org/wiki/MetaExtensions#Meta_Name_Extensions">1ส่วนขยายชื่อเมตา</a></li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><a href="https://wiki.whatwg.org/wiki/MetaExtensions#Proposals_That_Don't_Meet_the_Requirements_For_a_Registration">2ข้อเสนอที่ไม่ตรงตามข้อกําหนดสําหรับการลงทะเบียน</a></li>
<!-- /wp:list-item -->

<!-- wp:list-item -->
<li><a href="https://wiki.whatwg.org/wiki/MetaExtensions#Failed_Proposals">3ข้อเสนอที่ล้มเหลว</a></li>
<!-- /wp:list-item --></ul>
<!-- /wp:list -->

<!-- wp:heading -->
<h2 class="wp-block-heading">ส่วนขยายชื่อเมตา</h2>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:table -->

class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">สถานะ</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>AGLSTERMS.การเข้าถึง</td><td>ข้อความที่ระบุลักษณะการเข้าถึงของทรัพยากร</td><td><a rel="noopener" AGLS</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>AGLSTERMS.accessMode</td><td>โหมดการรับรู้สําหรับทรัพยากร</td><td><a rel="noopener" AGLS</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>AGLSTERMS.act</td><td>กฎหมายเฉพาะที่กําหนดหรือขับเคลื่อนการสร้างหรือการจัดหาทรัพยากร</td><td><a rel="noopener" AGLS</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>AGLSTERMS.aggregationLevel</td><td>ระดับการรวมตัวของทรัพยากรที่อธิบายไว้ - 'รายการ' หรือ 'การรวบรวม'</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.agls.gov.au/pdf/AGLS%20Metadata%20Standard%20Part%202%20Usage%20Guide.PDF" target="_blank">คู่มือการใช้งานข้อมูลเมตาของ AGLS</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>AGLSTERMS.allow-search</td><td>กฎหมายเฉพาะที่กําหนดหรือขับเคลื่อนการสร้างหรือการจัดหาทรัพยากร</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.agls.gov.au/pdf/AGLS%20Metadata%20Standard%20Part%202%20Usage%20Guide.PDF" target="_blank">คู่มือการใช้งานข้อมูลเมตาของ AGLS</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>AGLSTERMS.ความพร้อมใช้งาน</td><td>วิธีการรับหรือเข้าถึงทรัพยากรหรือข้อมูลติดต่อ ส่วนใหญ่ใช้สําหรับทรัพยากรออฟไลน์เพื่อให้ข้อมูลเกี่ยวกับวิธีการเข้าถึงทรัพยากรทางกายภาพ</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.agls.gov.au/pdf/AGLS%20Metadata%20Standard%20Part%202%20Usage%20Guide.PDF" target="_blank">คู่มือการใช้งานข้อมูลเมตาของ AGLS</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>AGLSTERMS.case</td><td>กฎหมายกรณีเฉพาะที่กําหนดหรือขับเคลื่อนการสร้างหรือการจัดหาทรัพยากร</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.agls.gov.au/pdf/AGLS%20Metadata%20Standard%20Part%202%20Usage%20Guide.PDF" target="_blank">คู่มือการใช้งานข้อมูลเมตาของ AGLS</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>AGLSTERMS.หมวดหมู่</td><td>ประเภททั่วไปของทรัพยากรที่กําลังอธิบาย - 'บริการ', 'เอกสาร' หรือ 'หน่วยงาน'</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.agls.gov.au/pdf/AGLS%20Metadata%20Standard%20Part%202%20Usage%20Guide.PDF" target="_blank">คู่มือการใช้งานข้อมูลเมตาของ AGLS</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>AGLSTERMS.dateLicensed</td><td>วันที่ใบอนุญาตถูกนําไปใช้หรือมีผลใช้บังคับ</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.agls.gov.au/pdf/AGLS%20Metadata%20Standard%20Part%202%20Usage%20Guide.PDF" target="_blank">คู่มือการใช้งานข้อมูลเมตาของ AGLS</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>AGLSTERMS.documentType</td><td>รูปแบบของทรัพยากรที่อธิบายไว้โดยที่ค่าของหมวดหมู่คือ 'document’ เอกสารถูกใช้ในความหมายที่กว้างที่สุดและรวมถึงทรัพยากรต่างๆ เช่น ข้อความ รูปภาพ ไฟล์เสียง และซอฟต์แวร์</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.agls.gov.au/pdf/AGLS%20Metadata%20Standard%20Part%202%20Usage%20Guide.PDF" target="_blank">คู่มือการใช้งานข้อมูลเมตาของ AGLS</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>AGLSTERMS.function</td><td>หน้าที่ทางธุรกิจที่เกี่ยวข้องกับทรัพยากร หน้าที่เป็นหน่วยหลักของกิจกรรมที่องค์กรต่างๆ ดําเนินการเพื่อให้บรรลุภารกิจและเป้าหมายขององค์กร</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.agls.gov.au/pdf/AGLS%20Metadata%20Standard%20Part%202%20Usage%20Guide.PDF" target="_blank">คู่มือการใช้งานข้อมูลเมตาของ AGLS</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>AGLSTERMS.isBasedOn</td><td>ทรัพยากรที่เกี่ยวข้องซึ่งทรัพยากรที่อธิบายไว้คือประสิทธิภาพ การผลิต ที่มา การแปล หรือการตีความ</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.agls.gov.au/pdf/AGLS%20Metadata%20Standard%20Part%202%20Usage%20Guide.PDF" target="_blank">คู่มือการใช้งานข้อมูลเมตาของ AGLS</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>AGLSTERMS.isBasisFor</td><td>ทรัพยากรที่เกี่ยวข้องซึ่งเป็นประสิทธิภาพ การผลิต การได้มา การแปล หรือการตีความทรัพยากรที่อธิบายไว้</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.agls.gov.au/pdf/AGLS%20Metadata%20Standard%20Part%202%20Usage%20Guide.PDF" target="_blank">คู่มือการใช้งานข้อมูลเมตาของ AGLS</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>AGLSTERMS.เขตอํานาจศาล</td><td>ชื่อของหน่วยงานทางการเมือง/การบริหารที่ครอบคลุมโดยทรัพยากรที่อธิบายไว้</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.agls.gov.au/pdf/AGLS%20Metadata%20Standard%20Part%202%20Usage%20Guide.PDF" target="_blank">คู่มือการใช้งานข้อมูลเมตาของ AGLS</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>AGLSTERMS.อาณัติ</td><td>เครื่องมือทางกฎหมายเฉพาะที่ต้องการหรือขับเคลื่อนการสร้างหรือการจัดหาทรัพยากร</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.agls.gov.au/pdf/AGLS%20Metadata%20Standard%20Part%202%20Usage%20Guide.PDF" target="_blank">คู่มือการใช้งานข้อมูลเมตาของ AGLS</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>AGLSTERMS.เครื่องหมายป้องกัน</td><td>เครื่องหมายป้องกันที่ใช้กับทรัพยากรที่อธิบายไว้</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.agls.gov.au/pdf/AGLS%20Metadata%20Standard%20Part%202%20Usage%20Guide.PDF" target="_blank">คู่มือการใช้งานข้อมูลเมตาของ AGLS</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>AGLSTERMS.ระเบียบข้อบังคับ</td><td>กฎระเบียบเฉพาะที่กําหนดหรือขับเคลื่อนการสร้างหรือการจัดหาทรัพยากร</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.agls.gov.au/pdf/AGLS%20Metadata%20Standard%20Part%202%20Usage%20Guide.PDF" target="_blank">คู่มือการใช้งานข้อมูลเมตาของ AGLS</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>AGLSTERMS.serviceType</td><td>รูปแบบของทรัพยากรที่อธิบายไว้โดยที่มูลค่าของหมวดหมู่คือ ‘service'</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.agls.gov.au/pdf/AGLS%20Metadata%20Standard%20Part%202%20Usage%20Guide.PDF" target="_blank">คู่มือการใช้งานข้อมูลเมตาของ AGLS</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>อเล็กซาเวอริฟายิด</td><td>ใช้ในการตรวจสอบความเป็นเจ้าของ Alexa Search</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.alexa.com/faqs/?p=188" target="_blank">คําถามที่พบบ่อยของ Alexa เกี่ยวกับแอตทริบิวต์เมตานี้ อ้างอิง</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>แอปเปิ้ล-itunes-แอป</td><td>การโปรโมตแอปด้วยแบนเนอร์แอปอัจฉริยะ</td><td><a
