the lifecycle of the resource represented in terms of ICAS long date format such as "UCN 12012 M03 Blue ❀ day 333 ❀ IDC zone(UT) t969 tt189". example &lt;meta name="icas.datetime.long" content="UCN 12012 M03 Blue ❀ day 333 ❀ IDC zone(UT) t969 tt189"/&gt;</td><td>a preliminary specification in the aaticas group on LinkedIn (<a rel="noopener" href="http://www.linkedin.com/groups/aaticas-4034149" target="_blank">http://www.linkedin.com/groups/aaticas-4034149</a>). after a period of review, a specification for AAT ICAS meta keywords for HTML(5) will be referenced on an AAT ICAS area of the aatideas.org web site.</td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>icbm</td><td>Defines geographic position to which page is related to. The acronym stands for ICBM Intercontinental Ballistic Missile - an old, humorous allusion to the possible use of such coordinates.<br>ตัวอย่าง:&lt;meta name="ICBM" content="47.0667, 15.4500" /&gt;<br>Like geo.position except for value syntax.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://geourl.org/add.html" target="_blank">GeoURL documentation</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>IE_RM_OFF</td><td>If set to "true", disables Internet Explorer 11 Reading View button adjacent to address bar when the page is detected to have content suitable for Reading View. This is intended for pages that are not articles and are not intended to be read in IE 11 Reading View.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://ie.microsoft.com/testdrive/browser/readingview/" target="_blank">Microsoft: Reading View Guidelines and Information</a>&nbsp;In the Code tab, at the bottom where Opt Out is read.</td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>itemsPerPage</td><td>Used to identify the number of search results returned per page.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.opensearch.org/Specifications/OpenSearch/1.1#Response_metadata_in_HTML.2FXHTML" target="_blank">OpenSearch Specification</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>keywords-not</td><td>Like keywords but instead keywords-not support Boolean NOT searches; useful for metaphors, satire/parody, and pages that say "this page is not about subject x" which therefore would be erroneously excluded by a NOT search against x.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://brittlebit.org/specifications/html-meta-keywords-not/specification-for-html-meta-element-with-name-value-keywords-not.html" target="_blank">ข้อมูลจําเพาะของ BrittleBit.org</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>license</td><td>Is used to to define the license under which a website is published.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="https://faq.frontendnet.work/metatags/Miscellaneous/license" target="_blank">Unofficial Documentation of license</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>license:uri</td><td>Is used to to define the uri to the license, under which a website is published.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="https://faq.frontendnet.work/metatags/Miscellaneous/licenseuri" target="_blank">Unofficial Documentation of license:uri</a></td><td></td><td>ข้อเสนอ</td></tr><tr><td>meta_date</td><td>The date used to indicate that the Metadata has been prepared and/or reviewed and approved by the Metadata Unit. Its purpose is administrative. (Used by "Autonomy".)</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.hc-sc.gc.ca/home-accueil/alt_formats/pacrb-dgapcr/pdf/Metadata_Application_Profile_2009.pdf" target="_blank">Health Canada Web Metadata Application Profile March 2009</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>microtip</td><td>Specifies the cryptocurrency address (bitcoin, etc.) for sending tips to the people responsible for creating this page's content. This tag is used by various browser plugins that want to compensate the site's creators. Since it's microtipping instead of micropayments, there is no need to specify an amount.<br>Example:&lt;meta name="microtip" content="[bitcoin-address]"&gt; Note: there can be multiple instances of this metatag on a page.</td><td>For full specification, see:&nbsp;<a rel="noopener" href="http://autotip.io/docs/microtip-meta-tag" target="_blank">http://autotip.io/docs/microtip-meta-tag</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>mobile-agent</td><td>Specifies the mobile-compatible url of the web page. Used by mobile browsers and search engines to redirect mobile phone visitors to the proper mobile page.<br>The following properties can be used in the value of the content attribute:<br>url - The mobile-compatible url of the web page.<br>format - The format of the mobile page. An enum of "wml", "xhtml" and "html5".<br>Example:&lt;meta name="mobile-agent" content="format=html5; url=<a rel="noopener" href="http://3g.sina.com.cn/" target="_blank">http://3g.sina.com.cn/</a>"&gt;</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://open.shouji.baidu.com/?page=developer&amp;action=pcandmo" target="_blank">Baidu Mobile SEO</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>mobile-web-app-capable</td><td>Sets whether a web application can be added standalone to a home screen and launched in fullscreen mode. Also proposed as a vendor-neutral version of apple-mobile-web-app-capable.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="https://developers.google.com/chrome/mobile/docs/installtohomescreen" target="_blank">Add to Homescreen - Google Chrome Mobile — Google Developers</a>&nbsp;(though a WHATWG or W3C spec would be preferred)</td><td>apple-mobile-web-app-capable (vendor specific synonym)</td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>mobileoptimized</td><td>Controls layout behavior in older versions of Internet Explorer (e.g., 6.5).</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/bb431690.aspx" target="_blank">Microsoft Windows Mobile 6.5 documentation</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>msapplication-config</td><td><strong>"Pinned Sites" in Windows 8</strong>The "msapplication-config" metadata defines the path to a browser configuration file, letting you set pinned sites customizations (such as tile background, badge updates and notifications) with this external XML file rather than metadata within the HTML markup of webpages.&lt;meta name="msapplication-config" content="IEconfig.xml" /&gt; <strong>Note</strong>&nbsp;Without this metadata, IE11 looks for a default "browserconfig.xml" in the root directory of the server.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/ie/dn320426%28v=vs.85%29.aspx" target="_blank">Browser configuration schema reference</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>msapplication-navbutton-color</td><td><strong>"Jump List" or "Pinned Sites" in Windows 7</strong>The "msapplication-navbutton-color" metadata define the custom color of the Back and Forward buttons in the Pinned Site browser window. Any named color, or hex color value is valid.&lt;meta name="msapplication-navbutton-color" content="#FF3300" /&gt;</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/gg491732(v=VS.85).aspx" target="_blank">Declaring Pinned Site Metadata</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>msapplication-notification</td><td><strong>"Pinned Sites" and "Live Tiles" in Windows 8.1</strong>Used to make pinned sites on the start screen into live tiles. Usage:&lt;META name="msapplication-notification" content="cycle=7;frequency=60;polling-uri=<a rel="noopener" href="http://www.example.com/notifications.xml" target="_blank">http://www.example.com/notifications.xml</a>" /&gt;</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="https://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/ie/dn255024.aspx#msapplication-notification" target="_blank">Pinned site metadata reference</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>msapplication-square150x150logo</td><td><strong>msapplication-square150x150logo</strong>Specifies the image to use as the wide tile, which is 310x150 pixels at 100% scaling.&lt;meta name="msapplication-square150x150logo" content="images/logo.png"&gt; <strong>Note</strong>&nbsp;The&nbsp;<strong>msapplication-square150x150logo</strong>&nbsp;value is supported as of IE11 Preview and applies to tiles pinned to the Windows Start screen.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/ie/dn255024%28v=vs.85%29.aspx" target="_blank">Pinned site metadata reference</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>msapplication-square310x310logo</td><td><strong>msapplication-square310x310logo</strong>Specifies the image to use as the large tile, which is 310x310 pixels at 100% scaling.&lt;meta name="msapplication-square310x310logo" content="images/largelogo.png"&gt; <strong>Note</strong>&nbsp;The&nbsp;<strong>msapplication-square310x310logo</strong>&nbsp;value is supported as of IE11 Preview and applies to tiles pinned to the Windows Start screen.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/ie/dn255024%28v=vs.85%29.aspx" target="_blank">Pinned site metadata reference</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>msapplication-square70x70logo</td><td><strong>msapplication-square70x70logo</strong>Specifies the image to use as the small tile, which is 70x70 pixels at 100% scaling.&lt;meta name="msapplication-square70x70logo" content="images/tinylogo.png"&gt; <strong>Note</strong>&nbsp;The&nbsp;<strong>msapplication-square70x70logo</strong>&nbsp;value is supported as of IE11 Preview and applies to tiles pinned to the Windows Start screen.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/ie/dn255024%28v=vs.85%29.aspx" target="_blank">Pinned site metadata reference</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>msapplication-starturl</td><td><strong>"Jump List" or "Pinned Sites" in Windows 7</strong>The "msapplication-starturl" metadata contains the root URL of the application. The start URL can be fully qualified, or relative to the current document. Only HTTP and HTTPS protocols are allowed. If this element is missing, the address of the current page is used instead.&lt;meta name="msapplication-starturl" content="./" /&gt;</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/gg491732(v=VS.85).aspx" target="_blank">Declaring Pinned Site Metadata</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>msapplication-tap-highlight</td><td><strong>Link highlighting in Internet Explorer</strong>The "msapplication-tap-highlight" meta tag can be used to disable automatic highlighting of tapped links in Internet Explorer. Applies to IE10 on Windows Phone 8 and IE11 on Windows 8.1.&lt;meta name="msapplication-tap-highlight" content="no" /&gt;</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/ie/bg182645%28v=vs.85%29.aspx#tapHighlight" target="_blank">Link highlighting</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>msapplication-task</td><td><strong>"Jump List" or "Pinned Sites" in Windows 7</strong>Jump List items act as entry points into the website even when the browser is not running. A Jump List can contain commonly used destinations and tasks. Some items apply to the whole site, and some apply only to specific users. For example, to add a single task called "Check Order Status" specify a meta element in the head of your webpage, as follows:&lt;META name="msapplication-task" content="name=Check Order Status; action-uri=./orderStatus.aspx?src=IE9; icon-uri=./favicon.ico" /&gt;</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/gg491725(v=vs.85).aspx" target="_blank">Tasks in Jump List</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>msapplication-TileColor</td><td>The "msapplication-TileColor" metadata define the background color of a tile in Pinned Sites in Windows 8. The tile color can be specified as a hex RGB color using CSS’s #rrggbb notation, via CSS color names, or by the CSS rgb() function.&lt;meta name="msapplication-TileColor" content="#d83434"/&gt;</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://blogs.msdn.com/b/ie/archive/2012/06/08/high-quality-visuals-for-pinned-sites-in-windows-8.aspx" target="_blank">High Quality Visuals for Pinned Sites in Windows 8</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>msapplication-TileImage</td><td>The "msapplication-TileImage" metadata define the path to an image to be used as background for a tile in Pinned Sites in Windows 8. Tile images must be square PNGs 144px by 144px.&lt;meta name="msapplication-TileImage" content="images/benthepcguy-144.png" /&gt;</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://blogs.msdn.com/b/ie/archive/2012/06/08/high-quality-visuals-for-pinned-sites-in-windows-8.aspx" target="_blank">High Quality Visuals for Pinned Sites in Windows 8</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>msapplication-tooltip</td><td><strong>"Jump List" or "Pinned Sites" in Windows 7</strong>The "msapplication-tooltip" metadata provides additional tooltip text that appears when you hover over the Pinned Site shortcut in the Windows Start menu or on the desktop.&lt;meta name="msapplication-tooltip" content="Channel 9 Podcasts" /&gt;</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/gg491732(v=VS.85).aspx" target="_blank">Declaring Pinned Site Metadata</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>msapplication-wide310x150logo</td><td><strong>msapplication-wide310x150logo</strong>Specifies the image to use as the medium tile, which is 150x150 pixels at 100% scaling.&lt;meta name="msapplication-wide310x150logo" content="images/widelogo.png"&gt; <strong>Note</strong>&nbsp;The&nbsp;<strong>msapplication-wide310x150logo</strong>&nbsp;value is supported as of IE11 Preview and applies to tiles pinned to the Windows Start screen.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/ie/dn255024%28v=vs.85%29.aspx" target="_blank">Pinned site metadata reference</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>msapplication-window</td><td><strong>"Jump List" or "Pinned Sites" in Windows 7</strong>The "msapplication-window" metadata sets the initial size of the Pinned Site window when it is launched for the first time. However, if the user adjusts the size of the window, the Pinned Site retains the new dimensions when it is launched again. The following properties can be used in the value of the&nbsp;<code>content</code>&nbsp;attribute:width - The window width in pixels. The minimum value is 800.height - The window height in pixels. The minimum value is 600.&lt;meta name="msapplication-window" content="width=1024;height=768" /&gt;</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://msdn.microsoft.com/en-us/library/gg491732(v=VS.85).aspx" target="_blank">Declaring Pinned Site Metadata</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>MSSmartTagsPreventParsing</td><td>Prevent a Microsoft browser from adding a certain kind of content to a page because users could be confused into thinking the added content was part of the original page. (It's likely only relatively few copies of the browser, a beta release never finalized, are still in the installed Win base, but the base is so large that even relatively few would still be a significant absolute quantity.)<br><br>This tag is used by Google:&nbsp;<a rel="noopener" href="https://support.google.com/customsearch/answer/2595557?hl=en" target="_blank">Custom Search Help: Meta Tags</a>, as accessed 1-8-16. And "&lt;meta content='true' name='MSSmartTagsPreventParsing'/&gt;" appeared (with internal quote marks as singles) in the source code for &lt;<a rel="noopener" href="http://googleblog.blogspot.com/2009/04/listening-to-google-health-users.html" target="_blank">[9]</a>&gt;, as accessed 4-27-09.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://brittlebit.org/specifications/html-meta-mssmarttagspreventparsing/specification-for-html-meta-element-with-name-value-mssmarttagspreventparsing.html" target="_blank">BrittleBit.org spec</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>msvalidate.01</td><td>One of the verification elements used by Bing.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://onlinehelp.microsoft.com/en-us/bing/hh204490.aspx" target="_blank">Bing Webmaster Tools</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>nonfiction</td><td>To reduce the "fake news" problem, this lets well-meaning page authors label satire and parody appropriately in metatags although unlabeled in content (not explicitly labeling being common in satire and parody). Characterize the principal content of the page as nonfiction, fiction, mixed, or other. This way, other websites and users needn't be fooled.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://brittlebit.org/specifications/html-meta-nonfiction/specification-for-html-meta-element-with-name-value-nonfiction.html" target="_blank">BrittleBit.org spec</a></td><td>fiction (antonym; see spec for usage)</td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>norton-safeweb-site-verification</td><td>Used to verify ownership of Website for Norton SafeWeb.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://safeweb.norton.com/help/site_owners#verification_tips" target="_blank">Norton SafeWeb Help Documentation</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>origin</td><td>The&nbsp;<em>origin</em>&nbsp;meta tag is used to indicate the original source of a document.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="https://doc.ohreally.nl/metatag-origin" target="_blank">The&nbsp;<em>origin</em>&nbsp;meta tag</a></td><td></td><td>Proposed</td></tr><tr><td>origin-trials</td><td>Used to enable experimental browser features for sites which are registered to participate in experimental trials.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="https://docs.google.com/document/d/1v5fi0EUV_QHckVHVF2K4P72iNywnrJtNhNZ6i2NPt0M/edit?usp=sharing" target="_blank">Experimental Framework Trial Token Structure</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>p:domain_verify</td><td>Used to register a site's domain with Pinterest as a "verified domain".Example: <code>&lt;meta name="p:domain_verify" content="5dd1c5f2db0ac0b521f08d56b4cd271b"&gt;</code></td><td><a rel="noopener" href="https://help.pinterest.com/entries/22488487-Verify-with-HTML-meta-tags" target="_blank">Pinterest Help Article</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>page-version</td><td>When a page uploaded two or more times with the same last-modification date (per dc.modified, dcterms.modified, or article:modified_time metatag), distinguishing different uploads even on the same date can be done with the page-version metatag.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://brittlebit.org/specifications/html-meta-page-version/specification-for-html-meta-element-with-name-value-page-version.html" target="_blank">BrittleBit.org spec</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>pingdom</td><td>Used by Pingdom monitoring services as a heartbeat verification. The heartbeat service will only consider the request successful if it contains the "pingdom" meta tag, with the content set according to a per-customer key.Example:<code>&lt;meta name="pingdom" content="6bh3nxnx"/&gt;</code></td><td></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>pinterest</td><td>Used to block pinterest from linking to content on the URL.Example:<code>&lt;meta name="pinterest" content="nopin"/&gt;</code></td><td><a rel="noopener" href="https://support.pinterest.com/entries/21101932-what-if-i-don-t-want-images-from-my-site-to-be-pinned" target="_blank">Pinterest Help Article</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>prism:alternateTitle</td><td>An alternate title or alternate headline for a resource that may be used in a table of contents, a popup etc. and can vary with platform.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.prismstandard.org/specifications/3.0/PRISM_Basic_Metadata_3.0.htm" target="_blank">PRISM Basic Metadata Specification V3.0</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>pro</td><td>Signify expertise as judged by an authority. Counterpart to pro-auth (which see). Useful to search engines and Web directories.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://brittlebit.org/specifications/html-meta-pro/specification-for-html-meta-element-with-name-value-pro.html" target="_blank">BrittleBit.org spec</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>pro-auth</td><td>Signify an authority, subject to search engine's or directory's judgment of importance and credibility (the judgment could be by a human working for a search engine), so the authority's listing of experts can carry weight, making search results more useful.<br><br>Counterpart to pro (which see). Supported also by pro-auth-field (which see) and pro-auth-fragment (which see).</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://brittlebit.org/specifications/html-meta-pro-auth/specification-for-html-meta-element-with-name-value-pro-auth.html" target="_blank">BrittleBit.org spec</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>pro-auth-field</td><td>Signify an authority's claimed field of expertise, importance and validity to be judged by search engine or directory. Supports pro-auth (which see).</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://brittlebit.org/specifications/html-meta-pro-auth-field/specification-for-html-meta-element-with-name-value-pro-auth-field.html" target="_blank">BrittleBit.org spec</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>pro-auth-fragment</td><td>Signify which links from authority are links to experts, as distinct from other links. Supports pro-auth (which see).</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://brittlebit.org/specifications/html-meta-pro-auth-fragment/specification-for-html-meta-element-with-name-value-pro-auth-fragment.html" target="_blank">BrittleBit.org spec</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>rating</td><td>The Restricted to Adults label (RTA) provides a way for adult oriented websites to indicate that their content is off limits to children. RTA was introduced in 2006 and is currently used by a large number of adult web content providers. RTA is recognized by all major parental control filters.Example:&lt;meta name="RATING" content="RTA-5042-1996-1400-1577-RTA" /&gt;</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.rtalabel.org/index.php?content=howto" target="_blank">RTA documentation</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>referrer</td><td>Controls whether the user agent includes the Referer header in HTTP requests originating from this document</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="https://www.w3.org/TR/referrer-policy/" target="_blank">Referrer Policy</a></td><td>referer</td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>RepostUsAPIKey</td><td>Used to verify ownership of Website for Repost syndication service</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.repost.us/meta-headers-used-by-repost/" target="_blank">Meta Headers used by Repost</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles</td><td><a href="http://www.mediawiki.org/wiki/">MediaWiki</a>'s&nbsp;<a href="http://www.mediawiki.org/wiki/ResourceLoader">ResourceLoader</a>&nbsp;uses this name with&nbsp;<code>content</code>&nbsp;set to the empty string. The purpose is to mark the DOM position before which dynamic styles should be added.</td><td><a href="http://www.mediawiki.org/wiki/ResourceLoader/ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles_specification">ResourceLoaderDynamicStyles</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>review_date</td><td>The date a resource is scheduled for review by content creator in order to determine if it should be archived, updated or retained as is.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.hc-sc.gc.ca/home-accueil/alt_formats/pacrb-dgapcr/pdf/Metadata_Application_Profile_2009.pdf" target="_blank">Health Canada Web Metadata Application Profile March 2009</a><a rel="noopener" href="http://lists.w3.org/Archives/Public/www-archive/2014Feb/att-0020/dnd_ims-6001-1-2-eng.pdf" target="_blank">IMS 6001-1-2, Metadata for Recordkeeping and Web Content</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>revision</td><td>The revision of this page as reported by an underlying Version Control System. This is a free format string.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="https://github.com/krallin/meta-revision" target="_blank">Meta Revision Specification</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>revisit-after</td><td>revisit-after is used to tell search engines how often to recrawl the page. To our knowledge only one search engine has ever supported it, and that search engine was never widely used — at this point, it is nothing more than a good luck charm.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://code.google.com/webstats/2005-12/metadata.html" target="_blank">Google documentation</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>rights</td><td>Assert legal rights for the page in a machine-parsable format. Rights could include those in copyright, for trade secrets, and in other intellectual property.<br><br><a href="https://wiki.whatwg.org/wiki/Talk:MetaExtensions#rights:_why_reversion">Talk</a></td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://brittlebit.org/specifications/html-meta-rights/specification-for-html-meta-element-with-name-value-rights.html" target="_blank">BrittleBit.org spec</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>rights-standard</td><td>The purpose is to enable search engines and other cataloging services to compile the types of rights allocated to the work. (Does any search engine actually implement this?&nbsp;<a href="https://wiki.whatwg.org/index.php?title=User:Hsivonen&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1">hsivonen</a>&nbsp;07:34, 14 July 2011 (UTC))<strong>This keyword does not provide, remove or alter any legal protections or designations.</strong>Format:&lt;meta name="rights-standard" content="element id;rights" /&gt;element id - the HTML Element ID of the item these rights apply torights - what rights are assigned to the item"pd" - Public domain"cc by-sa" - Creative Commons Attribution"cc by-nd" - Creative Commons NoDerivs"cc by-nc" - Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial"cc by-nc-sa" - Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-ShareAlike"cc by-nc-nd" - Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-NoDerivs</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://sites.google.com/site/metarightsstandard/" target="_blank">Spec</a></td><td>Redundant with&nbsp;<a rel="noopener" href="http://www.whatwg.org/specs/web-apps/current-work/multipage/microdata.html#licensing-works" target="_blank">Microdata vocabulary for licensing works</a>.</td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>robots</td><td>A comma-separated list of operators explaining how search engine crawlers should treat the content. This meta name is already supported by every popular search engine.<br>Possible values are "noarchive" to prevent cached versions, "noindex" to prevent indexing, and "nofollow" works as the link rel value with the same name.<br>The content value "NOODP" has been offered elsewhere, so I'm proposing it here. It blocks robots from using&nbsp;<a rel="noopener" href="http://www.dmoz.org/" target="_blank">Open Directory Project</a>&nbsp;descriptions of a website instead of Web pages' own meta descriptions. It may have been introduced by Microsoft.<br>The content value "NOYDIR" has been offered by Yahoo, so I'm proposing it here. It blocks Yahoo's robot from using the Yahoo directory's descriptions of a website instead of Web pages' own meta descriptions. Whether any other robot supports this is unknown but possibly no other search engine uses Yahoo's directory anyway.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.robotstxt.org/meta.html" target="_blank">About the Robots &lt;META&gt; tag</a>; NOODP value:&nbsp;<a rel="noopener" href="http://www.google.com/support/webmasters/bin/answer.py?hl=en&amp;answer=35264" target="_blank">Google</a>,&nbsp;<a rel="noopener" href="http://help.yahoo.com/l/us/yahoo/search/indexing/indexing-11.html" target="_blank">Yahoo</a>, NOYDIR value:&nbsp;<a rel="noopener" href="http://ysearchblog.com/2007/02/28/yahoo-search-support-for-noydir-meta-tags-and-weather-update/" target="_blank">Yahoo</a>, as accessed 4-28-09</td><td>baiduspider (for targeting Baidu or Baiduspider only)<br>citeseerxbot (for targeting CiteSeerX only)<br>googlebot (for targeting Googlebot only)<br>googlebot-mobile (for targeting Googlebot-Mobile)<br>ia_archive (for targeting Internet The following properties can be used in the value of the&nbsp;<code>content</code>&nbsp;attribute:widthheightinitial-scaleminimum-scalemaximum-scaleuser-scalableExamples:&lt;meta name="viewport" content="initial-scale=1.0"&gt; &lt;meta name="viewport" content="width=480, initial-scale=2.0, user-scalable=1"&gt;</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://dev.w3.org/csswg/css-device-adapt/#viewport-meta" target="_blank">CSS Device Adaptation</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>web_author</td><td>Credits the developer(s) responsible for the technical design of a website.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.metatags.info/meta_name_webauthor" target="_blank">Documentation</a></td><td><a rel="noopener" href="https://sites.google.com/site/metadesignerspec/" target="_blank">designer</a>&nbsp;- for visual presentation</td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>witget</td><td>Used to verify ownership for Witget.com.Example &lt;meta name="witget" content="XXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX"&gt;</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://support.witget.com/topic/435278-prostaya-ustanovka-skripta/" target="_blank">Witget manual</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>wot-verification</td><td>Used to verify ownership of WOT (Web Of Trust)</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.mywot.com/wiki/Verify_your_website" target="_blank">WOT's verify your site wiki page</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>wt.ac</td><td><strong>Advertising Click parameter</strong>When a visitor to your site clicks on an ad, that action is referred to as an Ad Click. The following META tag tracks advertising clicks:&lt;META NAME="WT.ac" CONTENT="name"&gt; Defines the name of the advertisement clicked to reach a particular web page. The Ad Click must contain an external redirect back to the client. The redirect needs to include the necessary code to generate a hit to the SDC server. You can designate multiple Advertising Clicks using semicolons. Examples:&lt;a href="file111.html?WT.ac=CONTENT111"&gt; &lt;a href="file222.html?WT.ac=CONTENT222"&gt; The name of the advertisement clicked to reach a particular web page. To capture this information with DCS, the Advertising Click must contain an external redirect back to the client. The redirect needs toinclude the necessary code to generate a hit to the DCS. The maximum length for each name is 64 bytes.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://product.webtrends.com/WRC/OnDemand/ResourceCenter/rc/Library/PDF/IGOD/WebTrendsAnalyticsOnDemandImplementationGuide.pdf" target="_blank">Webtrends Parameters</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>wt.ad</td><td><strong>Advertising View parameter</strong>Visitors often view advertisements that they do not necessarily click on. You can use On-Site Advertising to determine the number of visitors to your web site who view particular ads. With this feature you can produce advertising reports for each of your clients. If you are selling advertising space on your web site, for example, you can collect traffic statistics to help determine pricing schedules. The following META tag tracks advertising views:&lt;meta name="WT.ad" content="My content"&gt; An Ad View occurs when a visitor views a page containing an ad. An ad is a link or graphic that contains an Ad Click parameter in the query portion of it's URL.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://product.webtrends.com/WRC/OnDemand/ResourceCenter/rc/Library/PDF/IGOD/WebTrendsAnalyticsOnDemandImplementationGuide.pdf" target="_blank">Webtrends Parameters</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>wt.cg_n</td><td><strong>Name of the Content Group</strong>Used to configure the appropriate Webtrends advanced feature. These are just some of the more popular ones. These appear in your META tags. – showing you the web page, the source (meta tag), the log files entry and the subsequent WT report. Example:&lt;meta name="wt.cg_n" content="My content"&gt;</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://product.webtrends.com/WRC/OnDemand/ResourceCenter/rc/Library/PDF/IGOD/WebTrendsAnalyticsOnDemandImplementationGuide.pdf" target="_blank">Webtrends Parameters</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>wt.cg_s</td><td><strong>Name of Content Sub-Group</strong>Used to configure the appropriate Webtrends advanced feature. These are just some of the more popular ones. These appear in your META tags. – showing you the web page, the source (meta tag), the log files entry and the subsequent WT report. Example:&lt;meta name="wt.cg_s" content="My content"&gt;</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://product.webtrends.com/WRC/OnDemand/ResourceCenter/rc/Library/PDF/IGOD/WebTrendsAnalyticsOnDemandImplementationGuide.pdf" target="_blank">Webtrends Parameters</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>wt.mc_id</td><td><strong>Identifies the ID of the marketing campaign</strong>To attract new students, a university launches a marketing campaign by sending recruitment email to all graduating high school seniors in a metropolitan area. The email links to a special landing page in the university’s web site, containing the following META tag to track marketing campaigns. Example:&lt;META NAME="WT.mc_id" CONTENT="1X2GG34"&gt; You may use this parameter on the URL. Example:&lt;a href="link?WT.mc_id=1X2GG34"&gt; The Campaign ID 1X2GG34 represents recruits to be contacted by email</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://product.webtrends.com/WRC/OnDemand/ResourceCenter/rc/Library/PDF/IGOD/WebTrendsAnalyticsOnDemandImplementationGuide.pdf" target="_blank">Webtrends Parameters</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>WT.si_n</td><td><strong>Scenario analysis parameter - scenario name</strong>This defines a scenario name for the page or set of pages to be included in the scenario. This in turn produces a funnel type report in Webtrends. Example:&lt;meta name="WT.si_n" content="my_scenario_name"&gt;</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://product.webtrends.com/WRC/OnDemand/ResourceCenter/rc/Library/PDF/IGOD/WebTrendsAnalyticsOnDemandImplementationGuide.pdf" target="_blank">Webtrends Parameters</a>.</td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>WT.si_p</td><td><strong>Scenario analysis parameter - scenario step name</strong>This defines a scenario step name for the page or set of pages to be included in the scenario. This in turn produces a funnel type report in Webtrends. It works when paired with metedata tag name WT.si_n. Example:&lt;meta name="WT.si_p" content="my_scenario_step_name"&gt;</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://product.webtrends.com/WRC/OnDemand/ResourceCenter/rc/Library/PDF/IGOD/WebTrendsAnalyticsOnDemandImplementationGuide.pdf" target="_blank">Webtrends Parameters</a>.</td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>WT.si_x</td><td><strong>Scenario analysis parameter - scenario step number</strong>This defines a scenario step number for the page or set of pages to be included in the scenario. This in turn produces a funnel type report in Webtrends. It works when paired with metedata tag name WT.si_n, and as an alternative to Wt.si_p. Example:&lt;meta name="WT.si_x" content="my_scenario_step_number"&gt;</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://product.webtrends.com/WRC/OnDemand/ResourceCenter/rc/Library/PDF/IGOD/WebTrendsAnalyticsOnDemandImplementationGuide.pdf" target="_blank">Webtrends Parameters</a>.</td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>wt.sv</td><td><strong>Tracking Servers parameter</strong>If your site is hosted on multiple servers, a server cluster, or a server farm, and you want to evaluate the performance of your load balancer, Webtrends can track page views for each server. To do so, populate the following META tag on all pages on each server: Example:&lt;meta name="WT.sv" content="My Server"&gt; Defines the name of the machine that serves the web page. If you have two servers (Server1 and Server2), you would make two copies of the META tag and designate CONTENT=“Server1” for deployment to pages on the first server and CONTENT=“Server2” for deployment to the same pages on the second server. For a server farm, you can extract the value of the built-in server name and dynamically assign it to the META tag using server-side scripting. Example:&lt;meta name="WT.sv" content="Server1"&gt; &lt;meta name="WT.sv" content="Server2"&gt; An Ad View occurs when a visitor views a page containing an ad. An ad is a link or graphic that contains an Ad Click parameter in the query portion of it's URL.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="https://tagbuilder.webtrends.com/Help/Miscellaneous/AdSearch.aspx?keepThis=true&amp;TB_iframe=true&amp;height=450&amp;width=650" target="_blank">About WT.ad</a>.</td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>wt.ti</td><td><strong>Tracking Page Titles</strong>You may want to modify a page title before sending it to Webtrends in the following cases:You are dealing with dynamic content pages identified by URL parameters, and the page title represents the title of the base URL page rather than the dynamic content page.Unless you modify the page titles, all pages have the same title in the reports.All pages have been assigned the same title, for reasons of style or company policy.Even though URLs are displayed in addition to page title, the entire URL cannot be depended upon to distinguish one page from another. Use server-side scripts to change the title to something that reflects the content of the pages so that you can identify them in reports. Next, pass the customized page titles to Webtrends, using the following META tag:&lt;META NAME="WT.ti" CONTENT="title"&gt; Defines the name of the title for this page.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://product.webtrends.com/WRC/OnDemand/ResourceCenter/rc/Library/PDF/IGOD/WebTrendsAnalyticsOnDemandImplementationGuide.pdf" target="_blank">Webtrends Parameters</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>y_key</td><td>Used to verify ownership for Yahoo! Site Explorer</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://help.yahoo.com/l/us/yahoo/search/siteexplorer/siteexplorer-06.html" target="_blank">Yahoo! documentation</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>yandex-verification</td><td>Used to verify ownership for Yandex Webmaster.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://help.yandex.ru/webmaster/?id=995300#995356" target="_blank">Yandex Webmaster ownership verification</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>ZOOMCATEGORY</td><td>Category of page to be grouped in Wrensoft Zoom Search Engine.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.wrensoft.com/zoom/zoommeta.html" target="_blank">Wrensoft Zoom Meta Documentation</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>ZOOMDESCRIPTION</td><td>Alternative page description for Wrensoft Zoom Search Engine.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.wrensoft.com/zoom/zoommeta.html" target="_blank">Wrensoft Zoom Meta Documentation</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>ZOOMIMAGE</td><td>URL to image to be displayed alongside result in Wrensoft Zoom Search Engine.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.wrensoft.com/zoom/zoommeta.html" target="_blank">Wrensoft Zoom Meta Documentation</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>ZOOMPAGEBOOST</td><td>Page boost factor to increase or decrease the relevance of page in Wrensoft Zoom Search Engine.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.wrensoft.com/zoom/zoommeta.html" target="_blank">Wrensoft Zoom Meta Documentation</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>ZOOMTITLE</td><td>Alternative page title for Wrensoft Zoom Search Engine.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.wrensoft.com/zoom/zoommeta.html" target="_blank">Wrensoft Zoom Meta Documentation</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr><tr><td>ZOOMWORDS</td><td>Additional keywords to be indexed for Wrensoft Zoom Search Engine.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.wrensoft.com/zoom/zoommeta.html" target="_blank">Wrensoft Zoom Meta Documentation</a></td><td></td><td>Proposal</td></tr></tbody></table></figure>
<!-- /wp:table -->

<!-- wp:heading -->
<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Proposals That Don't Meet the&nbsp;<a rel="noopener" href="http://www.whatwg.org/specs/web-apps/current-work/multipage/semantics.html#other-metadata-names" target="_blank">Requirements</a>&nbsp;For a Registration</h2>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:paragraph -->
<p>Note that these proposals can be moved back to the registry table if the problems listed in the rightmost column of this table are addressed.</p>
<!-- /wp:paragraph -->

<!-- wp:table -->
<figure class="wp-block-table"><table class="has-fixed-layout"><thead><tr><th class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">Keyword</th><th class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">Brief description</th><th class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">Link to specification</th><th class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">Synonyms</th><th class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">Status</th><th class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">Registration requirement failure</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>blogcatalog</td><td>Used to verify ownership of Blog Catalog.com</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.blogcatalog.com/" target="_blank">Blog catalog site</a></td><td></td><td>Incomplete proposal</td><td>Claimed spec link is not a link to a spec</td></tr><tr><td>created</td><td>The datetime at which the document was created. The value is an ISO8601 date. The date MUST follow the&nbsp;<a rel="noopener" href="http://www.w3.org/TR/NOTE-datetime" target="_blank">W3C Profile of ISO 8601</a>&nbsp;with a granularity of "Complete date:" or finer. The&nbsp;<a rel="noopener" href="http://www.bbc.co.uk/guidelines/futuremedia/desed/previousversions/searchmetadata_vs_1_0.shtml#metadata" target="_blank">BBC</a>&nbsp;use this name.</td><td></td><td></td><td>Incomplete proposal</td><td>Lacks link to a spec</td></tr><tr><td>expires</td><td><code>meta name='expires'</code>&nbsp;defines the expiration date of the page. This can be used for web pages in preparation for an upcoming event, e.g. a registration form for an exposition or competition, or other cases with a pre-set date when the document will no longer be valid, e.g. a product offer in a special sale or a support page for a product known not to be supported anymore from a given time onward.Search engines should respond to this meta tag in a reasonable way, i.e. by removing the page from their main search results after the expiration date (possibly still returning the result in a special search for expired pages as long as the page exists and is not explicitly excluded via&nbsp;<code>robots.txt</code>&nbsp;or&nbsp;<code>meta name='robots'</code>&nbsp;etc.) or simply by indicating to the user that this result is out-of-date.The content attribute should define the expiration date in accordance with&nbsp;<a rel="noopener" href="http://www.w3.org/TR/NOTE-datetime" target="_blank">http://www.w3.org/TR/NOTE-datetime</a>&nbsp;. The meta tag should not be used for pages without expiration date. However, for historical reasons, search engines should also interpret other date formats where possible and should be prepared to find values such as "", "0", "no" and "never". Such non-date values are to be interpreted as no expiration date.Correctly formatted example:<code>&lt;meta name='expires' content='2012-12-31T23:59Z'&gt;</code>This tag is not to be confused with and has a different meaning than&nbsp;<code>meta http-equiv='expires'.</code></td><td></td><td></td><td>Incomplete proposal</td><td>Lacks link to a spec</td></tr><tr><td>fb:admins</td><td>Open Graph Protocol by Facebook developers</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://developers.facebook.com/docs/opengraph/" target="_blank">Facebook developers</a></td><td></td><td>Incomplete proposal</td><td>The spec specifies this to be a value of the property attribute--not a meta keyword</td></tr><tr><td>fb:page_id</td><td>Open Graph Protocol by Facebook developers</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://developers.facebook.com/docs/opengraph/" target="_blank">Facebook developers</a></td><td></td><td>Incomplete proposal</td><td>The spec specifies this to be a value of the property attribute--not a meta keyword</td></tr><tr><td>gm-gpx-v</td><td>Wordpress Plugin Google Maps GPX Viewer</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://wordpress.org/extend/plugins/google-maps-gpx-viewer/" target="_blank">Google Maps GPX Viewer</a></td><td></td><td>Incomplete proposal</td><td>Claimed spec link is not a link to a spec</td></tr><tr><td>google-translate-customization</td><td>Used to verify ownership for Website Translator. &lt;meta name="google-translate-customization" content="Your Website Code Goes Here. Generated When Adding The Google Translate Plugin To Your Site" /&gt;</td><td></td><td></td><td>Incomplete proposal</td><td>Lacks link to a spec</td></tr><tr><td>nextgen</td><td>Used for nextgen gallery plugin in wordpress</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.alexa.com/faqs/?p=188" target="_blank">Alexa FAQ About this meta attribute Reference</a></td><td></td><td>Incomplete proposal</td><td>Unrelated spec link</td></tr><tr><td>nibbler-site-verification</td><td>Used to verify ownership of Nibbler site</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://nibbler.silktide.com/" target="_blank">Nibbler site</a></td><td></td><td>Incomplete proposal</td><td>Claimed spec link does not link to a spec</td></tr><tr><td>og:description</td><td>Open Graph Protocol by Facebook developers</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://developers.facebook.com/docs/opengraph/" target="_blank">Facebook developers</a></td><td></td><td>Incomplete proposal</td><td>The spec specifies this to be a value of the property attribute--not a meta keyword</td></tr><tr><td>og:image</td><td>Open Graph Protocol by Facebook developers</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://developers.facebook.com/docs/opengraph/" target="_blank">Facebook developers</a></td><td></td><td>Incomplete proposal</td><td>The spec specifies this to be a value of the property attribute--not a meta keyword</td></tr><tr><td>og:site_name</td><td>Open Graph Protocol by Facebook developers</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://developers.facebook.com/docs/opengraph/" target="_blank">Facebook developers</a></td><td></td><td>Incomplete proposal</td><td>The spec specifies this to be a value of the property attribute--not a meta keyword</td></tr><tr><td>og:title</td><td>Open Graph Protocol by Facebook developers</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://developers.facebook.com/docs/opengraph/" target="_blank">Facebook developers</a></td><td></td><td>Incomplete proposal</td><td>The spec specifies this to be a value of the property attribute--not a meta keyword</td></tr><tr><td>og:type</td><td>Open Graph Protocol by Facebook developers</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://developers.facebook.com/docs/opengraph/" target="_blank">Facebook developers</a></td><td></td><td>Incomplete proposal</td><td>The spec specifies this to be a value of the property attribute--not a meta keyword</td></tr><tr><td>og:url</td><td>Open Graph Protocol by Facebook developers</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://developers.facebook.com/docs/opengraph/" target="_blank">Facebook developers</a></td><td></td><td>Incomplete proposal</td><td>The spec specifies this to be a value of the property attribute--not a meta keyword</td></tr><tr><td>property="og:*"</td><td>Metadata used by the Open Graph protocol (used by Facebook). Note: currently these are defined as: &lt;meta property="og.*" content="x"/&gt;</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://developers.facebook.com/docs/opengraph/" target="_blank">FAQ About the Open Graph protocol from Facebook.</a></td><td></td><td>Doesn't belong in this registry</td><td>Not a value to be used in the&nbsp;<code>name</code>&nbsp;attribute</td></tr><tr><td>resolutions</td><td>Authoring web sites to use resolution independent images that display beautifully on high-resolution displays should be made as easy as possible for developers and should not require JavaScript to accomplish.To accomplish this, I propose a new HTML Meta Tag,&nbsp;<code>resolutions</code>, that can be used to specify that high-resolution versions of images linked to from the page are available and that the browser should use them in place of the lower-resolution default images if it detects that a user is using a high-resolution screen. The resolutions meta tag lists the device-pixel ratios supported by images in the page.So, for example…<code>&lt;meta name="resolutions" content="2x"&gt;</code>… means that the developer is telling the browser that she has created 2x resolution images for the images linked to from the current page and named them with a @2x suffix.To illustrate, if her image tag is as follows…<code>&lt;img src="/images/flower.jpg" alt="A flower"&gt;</code>… then she has two image files under /images: the low-resolution default (flower.jpg), and a higher-resolution (200%) version named&nbsp;flower@2x.jpg.(This is the same naming convention already used by Apple in its Cocoa Touch framework for automatically loading in higher-resolution versions of images.)Based on the meta tag, if the browser detects that the user is running at a&nbsp;<code>min-device-pixel-ratio</code>&nbsp;of 2.0, it will automatically ask for the 2x version of the image (flower@2x.jpg) instead of the default image as specified in the image tag.Finally, so as not to flood external sites with high-resolution image requests, this functionality would only work for local images specified via relative links.<strong>Multiple resolutions</strong>The resolutions tag can also contain a list of supported device-pixel ratios so as to support even higher-resolution displays when and if they become available in the future.For example:<code>&lt;meta name="resolutions" content="2x, 4x, 8x"&gt;</code>In this case, the developer would provide 2x, 4x, and 8x versions of all images. So, in the running example, she would make flower.jpg,&nbsp;flower@2x.jpg,&nbsp;flower@4x.jpg, and&nbsp;flower@8x.jpg.<strong>Advantages</strong>The advantages of this approach are several:Makes it very simple for developers to support high-resolution displays like the iPhone 4's Retina screenDoes not require JavaScriptDoes not change the default way that things work (if the meta tag is not specified, the browser simply behaves as it always has).</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://aralbalkan.com/3355" target="_blank">Proposal for native browser support of high-resolution image substitution</a><a rel="noopener" href="http://aralbalkan.com/3331" target="_blank">How to make your web content look stunning on the iPhone 4’s new Retina display</a></td><td></td><td>Incomplete proposal</td><td>Lacks link to a spec</td></tr></tbody></table></figure>
<!-- /wp:table -->

<!-- wp:heading -->
<h2 class="wp-block-heading">Failed Proposals</h2>
<!-- /wp:heading -->

<!-- wp:table -->
<figure class="wp-block-table"><table class="has-fixed-layout"><thead><tr><th class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">Keyword</th><th class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">Brief description</th><th class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">Link to more details</th><th class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">Synonyms</th><th class="has-text-align-center" data-align="center">Status</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>bot-.&nbsp;.&nbsp;.</td><td></td><td><a href="https://wiki.whatwg.org/index.php?title=MetaExtensions&amp;diff=3727&amp;oldid=3705">Old MetaExtensions revision diff</a>&nbsp;&amp;&nbsp;<a href="https://wiki.whatwg.org/index.php?title=MetaExtensions&amp;diff=4088&amp;oldid=3824">edit</a></td><td></td><td>Unendorsed</td></tr><tr><td>cache</td><td>This doesn't actually work; use HTTP headers instead.<br>Value must be "public", "private", or "no-cache". Intended as a simple way to tell user agents whether to store a copy of the document or not. An alternate for HTTP/1.1's cache-control; for publishers without access to modifying cache-control.</td><td>none</td><td></td><td>Unendorsed</td></tr><tr><td>DC.</td><td>Dublin Core, maintained by Dublin Core MetaData Initiative (DCMI), is an extensive system with some overlap with non-DC names.<br>This reserves all strings that begin with DC and a dot.&nbsp;<em>Not true; DC-HTML doesn't use hardwired prefixes, but defines the prefixes using link/@rel="scheme.prefix"</em></td><td><a rel="noopener" href="http://www.dublincore.org/" target="_blank">DCMI</a></td><td></td><td>Unendorsed</td></tr><tr><td>dir-content-pointer</td><td>No longer useful. The function is better performed with HTML markup.</td><td><a href="https://wiki.whatwg.org/index.php?title=MetaExtensions&amp;diff=3727&amp;oldid=3705">Old MetaExtensions revision diff</a>&nbsp;&amp;&nbsp;<a href="https://wiki.whatwg.org/index.php?title=MetaExtensions&amp;diff=10213&amp;oldid=10212">edit</a></td><td></td><td>Unendorsed</td></tr><tr><td>no-email-collection</td><td>HTML5 prohibits URL-valued meta names. They should be rel keywords instead.<br>Intended to reference legal policy of web site indicating that harvesting of e-mail addresses on the site is not permitted and in violation of applicable laws such as the CAN-SPAM Act of 2003.</td><td><a rel="noopener" href="https://www.projecthoneypot.org/how_to_avoid_spambots_5.php" target="_blank">Project Honey Pot</a></td><td></td><td>Unendorsed</td></tr></tbody></table></figure>

# Understanding GitHub Code Search syntax

You can build search queries for the results you want with specialized code qualifiers, regular expressions, and boolean operations.

## About code search query structure

The search syntax in this article only applies to searching code with GitHub code search. Note that the syntax and qualifiers for searching for non-code content, such as issues, users, and discussions, is not the same as the syntax for code search. For more information on non-code search, see [About searching on GitHub](/en/search-github/getting-started-with-searching-on-github/about-searching-on-github) and [Searching on GitHub](/en/search-github/searching-on-github).

Search queries consist of search terms, comprising text you want to search for, and qualifiers, which narrow down the search.

A bare term with no qualifiers will match either the content of a file or the file's path.

For example, the following query:

```text
http-push
```

The above query will match the file `docs/http-push.txt`, even if it doesn't contain the term `http-push`. It will also match a file called `example.txt` if it contains the term `http-push`.

You can enter multiple terms separated by whitespace to search for documents that satisfy both terms.

For example, the following query:

```text
sparse index
```

The search results would include all documents containing both the terms `sparse` and `index`, in any order. As examples, it would match a file containing `SparseIndexVector`, a file with the phrase `index for sparse trees`, and even a file named `index.txt` that contains the term `sparse`.

Searching for multiple terms separated by whitespace is the equivalent to the search `hello AND world`. Other boolean operations, such as `hello OR world`, are also supported. For more information about boolean operations, see [Using boolean operations](#using-boolean-operations).

Code search also supports searching for an exact string, including whitespace. For more information, see [Query for an exact match](#query-for-an-exact-match).

You can narrow your code search with specialized qualifiers, such as `repo:`, `language:` and `path:`. For more information on the qualifiers you can use in code search, see [Using qualifiers](#using-qualifiers).

You can also use regular expressions in your searches by surrounding the expression in slashes. For more information on using regular expressions, see [Using regular expressions](#using-regular-expressions).

## Query for an exact match

To search for an exact string, including whitespace, you can surround the string in quotes. For example:

```text
"sparse index"
```

You can also use quoted strings in qualifiers, for example:

```text
path:git language:"protocol buffers"
```

## Searching for quotes and backslashes

To search for code containing a quotation mark, you can escape the quotation mark using a backslash. For example, to find the exact string `name = "tensorflow"`, you can search:

```text
"name = \"tensorflow\""
```

To search for code containing a backslash, `\`, use a double backslash, `\\`.

The two escape sequences `\\` and `\"` can be used outside of quotes as well. No other escape sequences are recognized, though. A backslash that isn't followed by either `"` or `\` is included in the search, unchanged.

Additional escape sequences, such as `\n` to match a newline character, are supported in regular expressions. See [Using regular expressions](#using-regular-expressions).

## Using boolean operations

Code search supports boolean expressions. You can use the operators `AND`, `OR`, and `NOT` to combine search terms.

By default, adjacent terms separated by whitespace are equivalent to using the `AND` operator. For example, the search query `sparse index` is the same as `sparse AND index`, meaning that the search results will include all documents containing both the terms `sparse` and `index`, in any order.

To search for documents containing either one term or the other, you can use the `OR` operator. For example, the following query will match documents containing either `sparse` or `index`:

```text
sparse OR index
```

To exclude files from your search results, you can use the `NOT` operator. For example, to exclude files in the `__testing__` directory, you can search:

```text
"fatal error" NOT path:__testing__
```

You can use parentheses to express more complicated boolean expressions. For example:

```text
(language:ruby OR language:python) AND NOT path:"/tests/"
```

## Using qualifiers

You can use specialized keywords to qualify your search.

* [Repository qualifier](#repository-qualifier)
* [Organization and user qualifiers](#organization-and-user-qualifiers)
* [Enterprise qualifier](#enterprise-qualifier)
* [Language qualifier](#language-qualifier)
* [License qualifier](#license-qualifier)
* [Path qualifier](#path-qualifier)
* [Symbol qualifier](#symbol-qualifier)
* [Content qualifier](#content-qualifier)
* [Is qualifier](#is-qualifier)

### Repository qualifier

To search within a repository, use the `repo:` qualifier. You must provide the full repository name, including the owner. For example:

```text
repo:github-linguist/linguist
```

To search within a set of repositories, you can combine multiple `repo:` qualifiers with the boolean operator `OR`. For example:

```text
repo:github-linguist/linguist OR repo:tree-sitter/tree-sitter
```

> \[!NOTE]
> Code search does not currently support regular expressions or partial matching for repository names, so you will have to type the entire repository name (including the user prefix) for the `repo:` qualifier to work.

### Organization and user qualifiers

To search for files within an organization, use the `org:` qualifier. For example:

```text
org:github
```

To search for files within a personal account, use the `user:` qualifier. For example:

```text
user:octocat
```

> \[!NOTE]
> Code search does not currently support regular expressions or partial matching for organization or user names, so you will have to type the entire organization or user name for the qualifier to work.

### Enterprise qualifier

To search for files within an enterprise, use the `enterprise:` qualifier. For example:

```text
enterprise:octocorp
```

This searches repositories owned by organizations in the `octocorp` enterprise. User-owned repositories are not included.

### Language qualifier

To narrow down to a specific language, use the `language:` qualifier. For example:

```text
language:ruby OR language:cpp OR language:csharp
```

For a complete list of supported language names, see [languages.yaml](https://github.com/github-linguist/linguist/blob/main/lib/linguist/languages.yml) in [github-linguist/linguist](https://github.com/github-linguist/linguist). If your preferred language is not on the list, you can open a pull request to add it.

### License qualifier

To filter repositories based on their license or license family, use the `license:` qualifier and the exact license keyword, for example `Apache-2.0`, `CC`, `MIT`.

```text
license:MIT
```

See [Licensing a repository](/en/repositories/managing-your-repositorys-settings-and-features/customizing-your-repository/licensing-a-repository#searching-github-by-license-type) for a list of license keywords.

### Path qualifier

To search within file paths, use the `path:` qualifier. This will match files containing the term anywhere in their file path. For example, to find files containing the term `unit_tests` in their path, use:

```text
path:unit_tests
```

The above query will match both `src/unit_tests/my_test.py` and `src/docs/unit_tests.md` since they both contain `unit_test` somewhere in their path.

To match only a specific filename (and not part of the path), you could use a regular expression:

```text
path:/(^|\/)README\.md$/
```

Note that the `.` in the filename is escaped, since `.` has special meaning for regular expressions. For more information about using regular expressions, see [Using regular expressions](#using-regular-expressions).

<br>

You can also use some limited glob expressions in the `path:` qualifier.

For example, to search for files with the extension `txt`, you can use:

```text
path:*.txt
```

<br>
To search for JavaScript files within a `src` directory, you could use:

```text
path:src/*.js
```

* By default, glob expressions are not anchored to the start of the path, so the above expression would still match a path like `app/src/main.js`. But if you prefix the expression with `/`, it will anchor to the start. For example:

  ```text
  path:/src/*.js
  ```

* Note that `*` doesn't match the `/` character, so for the above example, all results will be direct descendants of the `src` directory. To match within subdirectories, so that results include deeply nested files such as `/src/app/testing/utils/example.js`, you can use `**`. For example:

  ```text
  path:/src/**/*.js
  ```

<br>

You can also use the `?` global character. For example, to match the path `file.aac` or `file.abc`, you can use:

```text
path:*.a?c
```

<br>
To search for a filename which contains a special character like `*` or `?`, just use a quoted string:

```text
path:"file?"
```

Glob expressions are disabled for quoted strings, so the above query will only match paths containing the literal string `file?`.

### Symbol qualifier

You can search for symbol definitions in code, such as function or class definitions, using the `symbol:` qualifier. Symbol search is based on parsing your code using the open source [Tree-sitter](https://github.com/tree-sitter) parser ecosystem, so no extra setup or build tool integration is required.

For example, to search for a symbol called `WithContext`:

```text
language:go symbol:WithContext
```

In some languages, you can search for symbols using a prefix (e.g. a prefix of their class name). For example, for a method `deleteRows` on a struct `Maint`, you could search `symbol:Maint.deleteRows` if you are using Go, or `symbol:Maint::deleteRows` in Rust.

You can also use regular expressions with the symbol qualifier. For example, the following query would find conversions people have implemented in Rust for the `String` type:

```text
language:rust symbol:/^String::to_.*/
```

Note that this qualifier only searches for definitions and not references, and not all symbol types or languages are fully supported yet. Symbol extraction is supported for the following languages:

* Bash
* C
* C#
* C++
* CodeQL
* Elixir
* Go
* JSX
* Java
* JavaScript
* Lua
* PHP
* Protocol Buffers
* Python
* R
* Ruby
* Rust
* Scala
* Starlark
* Swift
* Typescript

We are working on adding support for more languages. If you would like to help contribute to this effort, you can add support for your language in the open source [Tree-sitter](https://github.com/tree-sitter) parser ecosystem, upon which symbol search is based.

### Content qualifier

By default, bare terms search both paths and file content. To restrict a search to strictly match the content of a file and not file paths, use the `content:` qualifier. For example:

```text
content:README.md
```

This query would only match files containing the term `README.md`, rather than matching files named `README.md`.

### Is qualifier

To filter based on repository properties, you can use the `is:` qualifier. `is:` supports the following values:

* `archived`: restricts the search to archived repositories.
* `fork`: restricts the search to forked repositories.
* `vendored`: restricts the search to content detected as vendored.
* `generated`: restricts the search to content detected as generated.

For example:

```text
path:/^MIT.txt$/ is:archived
```

Note that the `is:` qualifier can be inverted with the `NOT` operator. To search for non-archived repositories, you can search:

```text
log4j NOT is:archived
```

To exclude forks from your results, you can search:

```text
log4j NOT is:fork
```

## Using regular expressions

Code search supports regular expressions to search for patterns in your code. You can use regular expressions in bare search terms as well as within many qualifiers, by surrounding the regex in slashes.

For example, to search for the regular expression `sparse.*index`, you would use:

```text
/sparse.*index/
```

Note that you'll have to escape any forward slashes within the regular expression. For example, to search for files within the `App/src` directory, you would use:

```text
/^App\/src\//
```

Inside a regular expression, `\n` stands for a newline character, `\t` stands for a tab, and `\x{hhhh}` can be used to escape any Unicode character. This means you can use regular expressions to search for exact strings that contain characters that you can't type into the search bar.

Most common regular expressions features work in code search. However, "look-around" assertions are not supported.

## Separating search terms

All parts of a search, such as search terms, exact strings, regular expressions, qualifiers, parentheses, and the boolean keywords `AND`, `OR`, and `NOT`, must be separated from one another with spaces. The one exception is that items inside parentheses, `(` `)`, don't need to be separated from the parentheses.

If your search contains multiple components that aren't separated by spaces, or other text that does not follow the rules listed above, code search will try to guess what you mean. It often falls back on treating that component of your query as the exact text to search for. For example, the following query:

```text
printf("hello world\n");
```

Code search will give up on interpreting the parentheses and quotes as special characters and will instead search for files containing that exact code.

If code search guesses wrong, you can always get the search you wanted by using quotes and spaces to make the meaning clear.

## Case sensitivity

By default, code search is case-insensitive, and results will include both uppercase and lowercase results. You can do case-sensitive searches by using a regular expression with case insensitivity turned off. For example, to search for the string "True", you would use:

```text
/(?-i)True/
```
